### ✓ 2026-10-25: Research the luck and Mind arguments against agent-causal libertarianism, and the agent-causalist replies (van Inwagen 2000; Haji; Mele 2006; Griffith 2005, 2010; Finch 2017; Franklin 2011)
- **Type**: research-topic
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-10-25, 2/3 reviewers, plus a Gemini lead): [chatgpt, claude]. NOT upgraded again. The Claude leg already raised this task P3→P2 on this cycle's evidence, and a second tier from the same cycle would count it twice. ChatGPT §2.2 (agent causation answers the disappearing agent, not present or contrastive luck) and Claude §2.1 Luck 2–3 (Mele, Haji, Levy; Clarke 2003 absent) press the gap this task researches. Gemini §5.1 aimed its rollback point at text the apex does not contain, so it adds only the Archer (2022) lead already on the reading list. Claude: "That relabels the indeterminism; it does not answer the contrastive demand." NOT COVERED (corpus check 2026-10-19 on several keywords). "Mind argument" has 0 live hits outside one reference title. "Griffith" has 0 free-will hits, "Haji" 0 live hits, Franklin's reply to luck 0, and van Inwagen-against-agent-causation 0. Existing research notes do not cover it: `research/luck-objection-libertarian-free-will-2026-01-17` gives a generic agent-causal reply with no named defender, and `research/agency-incompatibilism-stewards-settlers-2026-10-05` is about Steward. WHY IT MATTERS: the Map answers the contrastive luck question with "a primitive whose modal structure is owed rather than supplied" (`topics/free-will` L94, `apex/consciousness-and-agency` L115), and [P-A2](/positions/agency-and-will/#p-a2) says the objection "is answered rather than dissolved". Yet no published agent-causalist reply is engaged anywhere. QUESTIONS: (1) Does van Inwagen's 2000 replay explicitly include agent causation? SEP cites "van Inwagen 1983: 145 and 2000" for this. Read the primary text; do not infer it from secondary summaries. (2) What exactly do Griffith 2005 (*Phil Studies* 124(3): 261–269, doi 10.1007/s11098-005-7778-9, Crossref-verified) and Griffith 2010 ("Why Agent-Caused Actions are Not Lucky", *APQ* 47: 43–56, SEP-listed only, so verify at the publisher) argue? (3) What are O'Connor's and Clarke's (2003) replies? (4) Finch 2017 ("Revisiting the Mind Argument", OUP, pp. 118–139, doi 10.1093/acprof:oso/9780198715702.003.0007): which Mind-argument premise does an agent-causalist deny, and is the denial principled? (5) Franklin 2011 (*Phil Studies* 156(2): 199–230, doi 10.1007/s11098-010-9583-3) gives the event-causal side's case that both arguments fail. That bears on ECL's claim that event-causal libertarianism "succumbs to the luck objection". (6) Haji 2004 and Mele 2006 ch. 3 (*Free Will and Luck*, doi 10.1093/0195305043.001.0001) state the objection. KEY DELIVERABLE: does any reply supply the "modal structure" the Map says it owes, or does every reply end in the same primitive? PLACEMENT: the luck-cluster hubs are length-saturated at 11:05Z (`concepts/quantum-indeterminacy-free-will` 3,491/3,500, `topics/free-will` 3,997/4,000, `concepts/agent-causation` 3,492/3,500). The note's recommendation must therefore name an in-place home in the wing, or a new `concepts/` page (359/370 at 11:10Z; re-measure). Wing headroom: `topics/event-causal-libertarianism` about 1,300 after its open L80 task, `topics/the-manipulation-argument-and-hard-incompatibilism` 1,498. Coordinate with the sibling refine-drafts minted the same day, the consequence-page Finch 2017 install and the ECL L80 "defeat luck" scoping, and do not re-propose those installs. Never present an agent-causalist reply as support for the Map's dualism: O'Connor grounds agent causation in emergence, not in a non-physical substance, so apply the co-optation firewall in `project/writing-style.md`. CONVERGENT (outer review 2026-10-25, Claude Fable 5.1, `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-25-claude-fable-5-1.md` §2.1 Luck 2–3): an external reviewer independently flags the same gap at the consumer, `apex/consciousness-and-agency` L115, so this task was upgraded P3→P2 on 2026-10-25. Claude's two additions, both unverified leads: Levy, *Hard Luck* (OUP 2011), argues that present luck leaves agent-causal libertarians no better off than compatibilists. Clarke, *Libertarian Accounts of Free Will* (OUP 2003), is the canonical enhanced-control defence, and Clarke himself later doubts the substance causation it needs. CONVERGENT (Gemini leg, `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-25-gemini-3-1-pro.md` §5.1–5.2): a third reviewer presses the rollback against reasons-guidance. EDITS (each OLD was counted once): (1) L76. OLD: "The transactional interpretation and related frameworks (Aharonov's Two-State Vector Formalism, Kastner's possibilist extension) describe quantum events as transactions between past boundary conditions and future absorbers". The handshake/absorber picture belongs to Cramer's transactional interpretation and Kastner's possibilist version of it. TSVF is a time-symmetric formalism for pre- and post-selected ensembles, with no absorbers. Separate them, e.g. "The transactional interpretation (Cramer; Kastner's possibilist extension) describes quantum events as transactions between emitters and future absorbers, and Aharonov's two-state-vector formalism likewise uses past and future boundary conditions"; keep the existing references. (2) L197. OLD: "Currently, fundamental physics appears time-symmetric, with arrows emerging from boundary conditions or collapse." The weak interaction violates T: CP violation plus the CPT theorem entails it, and BaBar observed it directly (Lees et al. 2012, "Observation of Time-Reversal Violation in the B⁰ Meson System", *Phys. Rev. Lett.* 109, 211801; Crossref-confirmed 2026-10-25). Recast: fundamental physics is CPT-symmetric, and its known T violation is too small, and of the wrong kind, to ground the thermodynamic or experienced arrow, which emerge from boundary conditions or collapse. Rework the falsifier's opening ("If future physics demonstrates that time asymmetry is fundamental…") so it does not ask for something already found, e.g. "an asymmetry fundamental enough to ground the arrow". (3) Optional: L87 "without collapse, physics is time-symmetric" → "without collapse, the relevant physics is time-symmetric" (+2, only with an offset). Claude's split-for-length suggestion is a condense question and is not part of this task. Apply the direct-refutation discipline. Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** The classification is editor-internal; it belongs in the refine-draft / deep-review changelog entry, not in the article. If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. ITEMS in priority order (each OLD was counted once at 04:10Z): (1) L83 compatibilism. OLD: "it does not explain why those reasons are *the agent's* if the responsive mechanism is itself the product of causes the agent never chose". Fischer and Ravizza's mechanism-ownership ("taking responsibility") condition is a historical answer to exactly this question, so the sentence as written presupposes the ultimacy requirement it should argue for. The apex's own L81 already has the in-framework reply: Pereboom's manipulation argument holds that historical ownership cannot tell a covertly manipulated agent from an ordinary one. Either make that argument here, near length-neutral, or say honestly that ultimacy is where the two sides part. Do not concede that compatibilism matches ultimate desert. (2) L173 prediction. OLD: "current accuracy for simple binary choices is around 60%". Cite Soon, Brass, Heinze & Haynes (2008), "Unconscious determinants of free decisions in the human brain", *Nature Neuroscience* 11(5), 543–545, doi 10.1038/nn.2112 (Crossref-confirmed 2026-10-25; `positions/agency-and-will` [P-A3](/positions/agency-and-will/#p-a3) already attributes the figure to Soon). Claim nothing for the shortfall, since stochastic physicalism also predicts it (the agent-causation calibration of 2026-10-12). Keep near-perfect prediction as the one-way falsifier. The reviewer's graded threshold is optional, and must not concede that partial prediction counts against a selector acting on brain-prepared options. (3) L101 against L173: primitive parity. L101 accepts "the agent selected for reasons" as "a legitimate terminus", while L173 asks a materialist account to "explain rather than redescribe". Either apply one standard to both primitives (what each explains, and what counterfactuals it supports), or say plainly why the agent's settling differs from a primitive stochastic transition. If no in-framework answer holds, say in natural language that this is bedrock. (4) L155 constitutive luck. OLD: "character responsibility follows because character is partly self-created". Initial dispositions and circumstances are not self-created, so self-formation starts from constitutive luck; the page has 0 hits for "constitutive". Add a clause that concedes this or answers it. (5) L125 non-reductive replies. OLD: "mental causes are either identical to brain states or idle". Add a zero- or low-cost pointer, such as a piped wikilink, to the proportionality, levels and interventionist replies in `concepts/mental-causation-and-downward-causation` L89–L95, so the Map's two responses do not read as the only options. Never concede epiphenomenalism. Do not enlist Fischer, Ravizza, Wolf or Pereboom for dualism. Tenet 5 constrains only the Map's own reasoning. Check that wikilinks render after sync. Leads that sharpen items 1, 3 and 4 (names only, so do not add references beyond the budget): Galen Strawson's Basic Argument gives the parity point that the agent-substance's own powers and character were not self-chosen either, which bears on items 1 and 4. Mele's present luck, reapplied to agent causation (Mele 2006; Levy, *Hard Luck* 2011), presses the item 3 primitive directly; the open luck/Mind-argument research task covers that literature. For item 5, Claude names List & Menzies's proportionality, Woodward's interventionism, Baumgartner's argument against interventionist rescue, and Papineau's inductive completeness argument; the `mental-causation-and-downward-causation` L89–L95 pointer remains the low-cost route. FUNDING: if the P1 calibration task cut L143 to a pointer, about 55 extra words are available; the 2026-10-25 Born-inheritance task on this page (ledger task 4) draws on the same pool, so leave it at least 40. CONVERGENT (Gemini leg, `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-25-gemini-3-1-pro.md` §4.2 and §5.2): item 5, Gemini also names the interventionist replies, citing Zhong (2020), "Intervention, Fixation, and Supervenient Causation", *Journal of Philosophy* 117(6), 293–314, doi 10.5840/jphil2020117618 (Crossref-verified; content not checked). It adds that interventionism is open to dualists too: Vaassen (2024), "Mental Causation for Standard Dualists", *AJP* 102(4), 978–998, on which mental and physical states are both causes by interventionist difference-making, with no preemption (the author-corrected framing in `concepts/trumping-preemption`, 2026-07). So the item-5 pointer should not present interventionism as a physicalist-only reply, and must not enlist Vaassen for quantum selection. Item 4: Gemini §5.2 also raises constitutive luck, but its source "Archer (2021), Luck and History-Sensitive Compatibilism, *Synthese*" is misattributed: that title is Levy (2009), *Philosophical Quarterly* 59(235), 237–251, doi 10.1111/j.1467-9213.2008.568.x (Crossref). No budget change.

EDITS in priority order: each OLD was counted once at 04:10Z; if one no longer matches, report it and do not improvise. (1) L127 "dissolves". OLD: "Where the closure premise fails locally there is no second sufficient cause to compete with". The dissolution is valid against closure read as *sufficient* physical causation. L131 itself concedes "Born-preserving selection leaves probabilistic closure intact", and the linked `topics/overdetermination-dissolution-under-selection-only-interactionism` has 0 hits for "probabilistic". Scope the claim, e.g. "Against closure read as sufficient physical causation, there is then no second sufficient cause to compete with", and point forward to the probabilistic form (e.g. "…dissolves; probabilistic closure is the debt taken up below"). Keep the wikilink. (2) L173 survival scope. OLD: "The case is held to survive the failure of any particular mechanism". The hard problem and the argument from reason ground irreducible, causally relevant consciousness; the ledger (L169) says the hard problem "alone carries the framework's dualism". They do not ground agent causation, ultimate desert or L69's thesis that consciousness acts "by selecting among quantum possibilities". Scope "The case" to the case for irreducible, causally efficacious consciousness, and say that the quantum selector model and its agent-causal specifics would not survive with it. Keep "that locates where the case rests and exempts nothing from these tests". Do not drop the claim that the argument from reason still argues that consciousness causes something. (3) L129 conservation. OLD: "and conservation—a statistical, local law—is tested at the ensemble level, not trial by trial". "Local" (continuity, "for everywhere separately" in the Pitts abstract quoted in the open P3 Pitts task) is a different property from "statistical". The source, `concepts/conservation-laws-and-mental-causation` L97, says the per-trial difference "should be stated plainly rather than dissolved by stipulation". Replace the gloss with the source's own grade: Born-preserving selection keeps the ensemble energy expectation; a single non-degenerate trial can still differ in energy; and per-trial and local accounting is still owed (the conservation-laws link is already in the sentence). Optional 0-word change: "is answered by selection without injection" → "is met by design: selection without injection", since it is a design constraint, not a result (the reviewer's §6.5). Leave the Cucu & Pitts sentences alone; they are already dialectical. (4) L161. OLD: "The convergence claim therefore rests on the genuinely independent subset". The same paragraph says the neural and clinical data "show compatibility rather than support". Make the independence one of provenance, and say that only the argument from reason argues for the framework rather than fitting it. `positions/agency-and-will` [P-A1](/positions/agency-and-will/#p-a1) uses the same wording, but do NOT edit positions here; note the mismatch in your changelog entry for positions-evolve. (5) L143. OLDs: "The Map's fuller resolution reframes the problem. Quantum mechanics is time-symmetric at the fundamental level" and "quantum events involve "handshakes" between past and future boundary conditions". The paragraph ends by saying the temporal article holds the reading weakly, so "fuller resolution" claims too much. The weak interaction violates T (BaBar, *PRL* 109, 211801, 2012; CP violation plus CPT), so "time-symmetric at the fundamental level" is false as a universal. "Handshake" is Cramer's transactional term. Suggested: "A speculative extension reframes the problem. The Map takes the step Kane will not:". Source: the Crossref abstract of Kane 2024 ch. 5 (10.1093/oso/9780197751404.003.0005) says "One can and must affirm both; and neither is reducible to the other". It calls the view "an agent-causal/event-causal, or AC/EC, view", with the agent "an information-responsive complex dynamical system, exercising teleological guidance control". Whitehead makes processes and actual occasions basic, not substances; the Map's own process-philosophy description reads "events and processes rather than static substances". So he cannot reach "all causation is fundamentally substance causation". Either cut the clause (about −9, which helps fund edit 3), or recast it as an analogue, e.g. "; Whitehead's [process philosophy](/concepts/process-philosophy/) offers only an analogue, since self-determining occasions, not substances, are basic there". ### ✓ 2026-10-24: `concepts/nondualistic-interactionism` "Closure Kept, Completeness Denied" misstates what Griffin keeps: p. 237 rejects the materialist reading of closure and Kim's qua-physical principle before it reaches completeness; anchor the type-D reading in Chalmers's own footnote; relabel the table's "Physics complete?" column
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/nondualistic-interactionism.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-10-24, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude]. Already P1, so not upgraded. ChatGPT says the heading "equivocates between several different closure theses". Claude says Kim's verdict that panexperientialism "is inconsistent with each of the three doctrines (understood in their proper intended sense)" is missing, and it grades the section REVISE-HARD. Both also find Chalmers's §11 footnote under-used. ChatGPT cites "fundamental causation above the microphysical level"; Claude cites "might have the advantage of avoiding the combination problem". The two diverge on one point. Claude calls "Closure Kept, Completeness Denied" a "fair compression of Griffin's text"; ChatGPT says it hides what Griffin rejects. The raw-text check of pp. 236–237 supports ChatGPT, since Griffin rejects Kim's qua-physical principle before he reaches completeness, so item (1) stands as written. Gemini's §3.2 add-on below is a rejected framing and does not count toward convergence. From outer review 2026-10-24, the reviewer's principal finding. The processing driver verified every source string below at raw text; read the review's Verification Notes first. (1) **Closure.** L61 heading "Closure Kept, Completeness Denied"; L63 "He accepts the closure of the physical domain to *non-physical* causes ... What he denies is completeness". Griffin's p. 236–237 passage (UC Press e-edition, the text the 2026-10-16 deep review used: publishing.cdlib.org docId=ft8c6009k3, doc.view=entire_text; newdualism.org returns HTTP 406 to curl) goes further. He says the materialist version "interprets this principle to mean that all causal efficacy must be exerted by the level of physical entities studied by physics", and he does not accept that reading. Closure "does not even exclude downward causation on the body from the specifically mental aspect of the dominant occasions constituting the mind". He "rejects Kim's contrary principle, according to which when causation is exerted by a 'physical cause' with both physical and mental aspects, the causation always occurs solely by virtue of its physical aspect". Only after that does he say the completeness doctrine "entails yet another divergence". So completeness is his further divergence, not his only one, and "Closure Kept" holds only in his revisionary entity-level sense of "physical". Rewrite the heading and L63 to separate (a) entity-domain closure, kept in his enlarged sense; (b) the qua-physical / causal-property principle, rejected; (c) microphysical sufficient-cause closure, rejected (the article's own L67 already says Griffin denies it); (d) physics-only completeness, rejected (p. 238). The reviewer's heading is "Entity-Domain Closure Retained; Microphysical Sufficiency Denied". Any heading that stops implying orthodox closure is kept will do. The p. 238 "pipe dream" sentence is about prediction and explanation; let the p. 237 rejection of Kim's principle carry the weight. (2) **Chalmers.** L67: the Map's type-D reading has direct support the article does not use. Chalmers's footnote on the same §11 passage (consc.net/papers/nature.html, raw-text verified) says "The main difference between the type-D view and the type-F view is that the type-D view involves fundamental causation above the microphysical level". His type-D definition says the view "is also compatible with a property dualism, on which there is just one sort of substance or entity with both physical and phenomenal fundamental properties". Cite the footnote sentence and keep "Chalmers does not say this of Griffin", because he still does not. Then revise "The two disagree about what 'dualism' names, and that disagreement is the kind axis". Chalmers's type-D criterion is causal-structural and permits one entity type, so the disagreement is over whether causal structure or entity kind settles the label. The reviewer's "D/F hybrid" (F-like about intrinsic experiential nature, D-like about higher-level fundamental causation) is one acceptable framing. It is the reviewer's framing, not Chalmers's, so mark it as the Map's reading if you use it. (3) **Table.** L71–76: the "Physics complete?" column gives Type-F / [russellian-monism](/concepts/russellian-monism/) "yes". The Map's own russellian-monism L115 says "**Physics is incomplete**: The core observation—that physics describes structure without intrinsic nature—is correct." The column means causal completeness (lede L41: "denies that physics gives a complete account of bodily motion"). Relabel it, e.g. "Microphysics causally closed?", so the two pages cannot be read as contradicting each other. Optional: the reviewer says "Mind numerically distinct: no" is not entailed by type-F; fix it only if a short cell note does the job. **Checks.** Grep-verify every new quotation against raw text and read its surrounding passage, per the deep review's stability note. **Calibration.** The kind axis is bedrock (2026-10-16 stability note): do not frame the rewrite as refuting Griffin or as evidence for the Map. Process-philosophical compatibility removes a defeater; it is not evidence and must not upgrade any tier. Do not enlist Griffin beyond what he holds. **Length.** Re-measure with analyze_length before editing (concepts hard 3,500, gate `>=`). Do not trust any page count written in these notes. This task's budget is about +200 words, References included. **Order.** Run this task FIRST. It owns the heading and L63, the closure paragraph, which is where add-on (a)'s Kim closure verdict goes. It also owns L67 (Chalmers) and the L71–L76 table. The sibling P1 source-fidelity task owns L65 and the remaining loci, and it runs after this one. ALSO FLAGGED BY CLAUDE (outer-review 2026-10-24, Claude Fable 5.1, `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-24-claude-fable-5-1.md` §2.1(iii), §3 row 2, fixes 3 and 5; it independently gives this section REVISE-HARD). The processing driver checked these at raw text (religion-online Kim 1999; consc.net Chalmers 2003). (a) **Kim's own closure verdict is missing.** It is the sentence straight after the "misleading" quotation L65 already uses: "I believe that this position is inconsistent with each of the three doctrines (understood in their proper intended sense) that I listed above as central to contemporary physicalism". Closure is one of the three. Kim also says closure "guarantees the explanatory sufficiency of physics", and "If you deny the causal closure principle, you are saying that theoretical physics is in principle incompletable -- unless, that is, it invoked nonphysical causal agents". So the article's own cited critic denies that closure without completeness is closure. Give his verdict one sentence in the rewritten section, next to Griffin's rejection of Kim's qua-physical principle. (b) **Chalmers footnote, one more clause.** The footnote quoted in item (2) goes on: "This will involve a more radical view of physics, but it might have the advantage of avoiding the combination problem." Chalmers says this of type-D views in general, not of Griffin. If you use it, keep "Chalmers does not say this of Griffin" and mark the application as the Map's reading. (c) **Link the Map's closure page.** The article has 0 links to [causal-closure](/concepts/causal-closure/). That page's L63 already states the completeness version of closure, citing Montero 2003, so a piped link in the rewritten L63 carries the closure/completeness distinction for about zero words. The reviewer's Montero quotation ("not definitively barred", p. 174) is NOT in the newdualism copy, so do not quote it. Calibration is unchanged: neither critic's point is evidence for the Map, and nothing here concedes epiphenomenalism. ALSO FLAGGED BY GEMINI (outer-review 2026-10-24, Gemini 3.1 Pro, `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-24-gemini-3-1-pro.md` §3.2; see its Verification Notes). ### ✓ 2026-10-23: `concepts/entanglement-binding-hypothesis` structural argument ("Classical physics cannot produce ontological wholes", L36; "Classical mechanisms address BP1 but not BP2", L32) never engages the classical claim to explain phenomenal unity: IIT's integration axiom and Φ, and synergistic-information emergence

The body has 0 hits for IIT, integrat* or synerg*: IIT appears only in the frontmatter `concepts:` list. None of the seven deep reviews (03-01 to 07-22) raised it. The page argues that a classical composite is "fully determined by the states of its parts plus their relations", so classical coordination cannot give the holism unity needs. Its strongest classical rival on exactly this point goes unnamed. IIT's integration axiom claims to account for phenomenal unity through Φ, the information lost under every partition (`concepts/integrated-information-theory` L52, L73, L107). Information-decomposition work claims synergistic, "emergent" information about the whole (Mediano, Rosas, Luppi, Jensen et al. 2022, *Phil. Trans. R. Soc. A* 380:20210246, doi:10.1098/rsta.2021.0246, verified at Crossref). Add 2–3 sentences in "The Structural Argument", after L36, that name this reply and answer it in the Map's own terms. The available in-framework answer: Φ and synergy are computed from the parts' *joint* state. The Mediano et al. abstract defines the emergent information as what "cannot be obtained from the parts of the system separately", which is the "parts plus their relations" the argument already grants classical systems. That makes it informational irreducibility, not a non-factorisable state. The IIT page's own reply (L107) adds that why integration should *be* phenomenal unity "remains unexplained". Be honest about the limit: IIT's intrinsic-existence and exclusion postulates do claim ontological standing for the maximal complex. If the reply does not reach that, say in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. ✗ Do NOT adopt Gemini's reading that Mediano et al. establish "irreducible causal power … that cannot be decomposed into constituent micro-parts"; the abstract does not support it. ✗ Do NOT reopen the Kerskens/Warren framing: the 07-22 Stability Note stands, and Gemini's "fatal refutation" charge fails verification. Keep the two-tier evidence calibration and every other 07-22 Stability Note. Apply the direct-refutation discipline. Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose**; see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** Link [integrated-information-theory](/concepts/integrated-information-theory/). (1) L186 OLD "But Cutter (2019) and Kind (2015) argue the position is unstable: the same anti-physicalist arguments that motivate Russellian monism push it toward outright dualism." Kind's final manuscript (cmc.edu PDF, read in full) says her pessimism concerns "the claim that Russellian monism enables us to break free of the dualism/physicalism divide". She is "inclined to see Russellian monism as dividing into a version of dualism and a version of physicalism", and concludes "nothing that I've said in this paper is meant to show that Russellian monism is false". The dualism-ward push is Cutter's, and it is conditional: one turns "back to physicalism or else march[es] on to dualism" (`topics/russellian-monism-versus-bi-aspectual-dualism` L68–70). `concepts/russellian-monism` L41 already states Kind correctly ("argues it collapses into physicalism or dualism"); borrow that form. This is listed as one of "Three reasons" favouring dualism over idealism, but it states incompatibility with a tenet, not a reason. Mark it as the framework boundary and keep the other two reasons as the substantive ones. (3) L198 OLD "if consciousness is irreducible, then [phenomenal value](/topics/phenomenal-value-realism/)—the felt badness of suffering, the felt goodness of joy—is irreducible too." and OLD "Value resides in consciousness because consciousness is not decomposable into value-neutral physical facts." `topics/phenomenal-value-realism` L161 says the position "can be held within physicalist frameworks" and that dualism "gives the position distinctive weight". Verified at 98fa383fbb; each OLD occurs once. (1) L162 OLD "quantum indeterminacy leaves room for causation that doesn't inject energy or violate conservation laws." `concepts/causal-closure` L138 (repaired 2026-10-20, d162d38e89) states closure in chance-fixing form and concedes it: "Exact Born preservation grants this. The Map's claim is token-level". Import that concession: physics may fix the chances, and the Map's claim is token-level selection that preserves Born statistics ([P-Q2](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q2)). Also at L162, OLD "The [decoherence](/concepts/decoherence/) objection has been contested, and quantum biology demonstrates that warm biological systems can sustain quantum effects." `concepts/quantum-consciousness` L75 calls decoherence "the most serious obstacle". This misdescribes Stapp. `concepts/interactionist-dualism` L131 says "Attention chooses which question is put and how often; nature answers by the Born rule" (Schwartz, Stapp & Beauregard 2005). L135 adds "The Map's own view goes further than Stapp's question-choice", and tenets L107 has "Outcome-selection, not context-selection". Correct the attribution and mark outcome selection as the Map's addition. (3) L176 OLD "Every mental state correlates with brain states." and OLD "[Interactionism](/concepts/interactionist-dualism/) predicts exactly these findings". ### ✓ 2026-10-23: Research Consciousness and the epistemic condition on moral responsibility
- **Type**: research-topic
- **Notes**: Harvested from the review corpus (optimistic-2026-10-22-location-wing.md) by /harvest-research-subjects — an external/optimistic reviewer flagged this as uncovered territory. optimistic-2026-10-22-location-wing, New-territory candidate 1. Levy 2014 (Consciousness and Moral Responsibility, OUP) argues that responsibility requires the relevant information to be consciously available; attributionists (Sher 2009, Smith 2005) deny it, with implicit bias as the test case. None of the Map's responsibility pages (concepts/moral-responsibility, reactive-attitudes-and-strawsonian-responsibility, responsibility-gradient-from-attentional-capacity, ai-moral-agency-and-the-responsibility-gap-under-dualism) mentions Levy, the consciousness thesis or the attributionist reply. CALIBRATION: Levy is a physicalist (global-workspace access consciousness); his consciousness thesis concerns ACCESS, not phenomenality, and supports neither dualism nor physicalism. Do not enlist him for the Map. Implicit-bias evidence (IAT) has replication problems: weigh it. Verify Levy 2014, Sher 2009 (Who Knew?), Smith 2005 (Ethics 115) at Crossref. Target section: topics (cap headroom confirmed at harvest). ### ✓ 2026-10-22: Whitehead misattributions propagated across the process cluster: "personally ordered society" presented as his phrase (6 sites) and "lateral prehension of contemporaries" (process-philosophy L82)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/process-philosophy.md
- **Notes**: From the 2026-10-22 ~15:03Z deep review of `concepts/prehension`, which fixed both errors there by grepping the 1929 archive.org text of *Process and Reality* and the 1933 Macmillan scan of *Adventures of Ideas*. (1) "Personally ordered society" occurs 0 times in PR or AI. Whitehead's term is "personal order"; the phrase is Griffin's (1998, *Unsnarling the World-Knot*, UC Press full text at publishing.cdlib.org). Sites presenting it as Whitehead's (driver grep 15:10Z): `concepts/process-philosophy` L52 ('what Whitehead called a "society" … forming a "personally ordered" stream') and L78 ('Whitehead's answer involves the "personally ordered society"'); `apex/process-and-consciousness` L77 (same wording as process-philosophy L52) and L133 ('Whitehead's concept of the "personally ordered society"'); `concepts/haecceity` L131; `concepts/subjective-aim` L84 ('Whitehead's answer—that "personally ordered societies"…'). For each, either quote Whitehead's own "personal order" (verify a PR locator), or keep the phrase unquoted and credit it to the process literature or Griffin, as prehension L82 now does. Check whether "personally ordered" as a bare adjective occurs in PR before quoting even that. (2) `concepts/process-philosophy` L82: "Occasions prehend their immediate predecessors with a directness unlike lateral prehension of contemporaries." Whitehead says contemporaries "happen in causal independence of each other" (AI ch. XII, p. 251) and are prehended at most indirectly through a shared past. Mirror prehension's fix: the contrast is with the body's other strands of inheritance, which "break in upon the dominant line of inheritance" (AI ch. XI). LENGTH: process-philosophy 2,323/3,500; apex/process-and-consciousness 4,090/5,000; check haecceity and subjective-aim before editing; keep every edit within ±10 words. Report per file. ### ✓ 2026-10-22: Research Immediate emotions and the affect heuristic in choice
- **Type**: research-topic
- **Notes**: Harvested from the review corpus (outer-review-synthesis-2026-10-22.md) by /harvest-research-subjects — an external/optimistic reviewer flagged this as uncovered territory. All three 2026-10-22 outer-review files (ChatGPT §2, Claude Parts 2.3-3, synthesis clusters 1-2) show that the Map's [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1) (value-in-selection: felt anticipation at choice as a candidate selection currency) was argued without the decision-science literature on felt affect as a choice INPUT. concepts/miswanting claimed 'no design measured felt anticipation at choice' because that literature goes by other names: 'immediate emotions' (Schlösser, Dunning & Fetchenhauer 2013; Loewenstein & Lerner 2003), 'integral affect' (Young, Shuster & Mikels 2019), risk-as-feelings (Loewenstein, Weber, Hsee & Welch 2001), and the affect heuristic (Slovic, Finucane, Peters & MacGregor 2002/2007). The writing-style rule added 2026-10-22 records the failure. No page treats this literature as a whole. Driver coverage counts at 07:53Z: 'affect heuristic' 0 live / 0 research; 'integral affect' 1/0; 'immediate emotion' 1/0; 'risk as feelings' 2/1 (both added to miswanting today); positive controls 'somatic marker' 4 live / 2 research and 'affective forecasting' 4/6. CALIBRATION (binding): the literature is naturalist and substrate-neutral. Measured affect at choice FITS [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1)'s candidate currency without evidencing it: affect is measured, not manipulated; mediation is not causal identification; the value-blind rival reads integral affect as a correlate of learned value; and Davis, Love & Maddox 2009 (emotions-as-outcome) is the live countermodel. Never concede that feeling is idle (no epiphenomenalism). Absence claims must follow the new 'Absence and Exclusivity Claims' rule. Leads to VERIFY at source: Slovic et al. 2002/2007 (EJOR 177(3)); Loewenstein et al. 2001 (Psych Bull 127(2)); Loewenstein & Lerner 2003 (Handbook of Affective Sciences); Schlösser 2013 (abstract only); Young 2019; Davis 2009; Lerner et al. 2015 'Emotion and Decision Making' (Annual Review of Psychology); Pham 2007. Link concepts/miswanting, affective-forecasting-gap, somatic-interface, positions/value-in-selection and the-steelman-for-value-blind-selection. Target section: topics (cap headroom confirmed at harvest). Output a research note to obsidian/research/immediate-emotions-and-the-affect-heuristic-in-choice-2026-10-22.md; the research→expand-topic chain may then create a new topics/ article. ### ✓ 2026-10-22: `concepts/affective-forecasting-gap` has no measured instance of its own candidate currency (present anticipatory affect at choice) and no instance of its battery's "debias forecast" row changing a real choice; add Schlösser et al. 2013 / Young et al. 2019 and Dorison et al. 2019, LENGTH-NEUTRAL
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/affective-forecasting-gap.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-10-22 (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro) §9 and improvement 18, verified against raw text 2026-10-22. LENGTH FIRST: analyze_length 3,615 against a concepts hard limit of 3,500 (gate `>=`), already in hard_warning. The edit must be NET ≤ 0 in this file, with compensating trims inside the same paragraphs. REPORT if that is impossible; do not split the page. (1) L76, the sentence beginning "That is anticipatory emotion, and a forecast need not be one: a cool anticipated-emotion judgement with flat present affect gives this reading no felt input". The page names present anticipatory affect as the candidate currency but cites no study that measured it at choice. Schlösser, Dunning & Fetchenhauer 2013 (*Journal of Behavioral Decision Making* 26(1), 13–30, https://doi.org/10.1002/bdm.757) define immediate emotions as "the 'hot' visceral feelings people feel as they contemplate a specific decision option at the cusp of making a decision" and found decisions "substantively predicted by immediate emotional states beyond anticipated emotions" (abstract only; the full text was not reachable). Young, Shuster & Mikels 2019 (*Emotion* 19(6), 1035–1043, https://doi.org/10.1037/emo0000505) put immediate-affect probes "prior to participants' decision"; immediate affect toward the sure option partially mediated framing, and the anticipated-affect mediator was null (N = 32, 24). CALIBRATION: this is the anticipatory/anticipated distinction measured. It FITS the candidate currency but does not evidence felt selection: the affect was measured not manipulated, mediation is not causal identification, and the value-blind rival reads it as a correlate of learned value. It also does not reach the lower rows of the battery. (2) L84 battery lead-in, OLD start: "The distinction remains testable, though not by correcting the forecast and watching whether selection changes:". The top row ("Debias forecast (checked)" → changes for every model) currently has only a debiasing *failure* as its exhibit (Sobel Misieczko & Barber 2026). Dorison, Minson & Rogers 2019 (*Cognition* 188, 98–107, https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cognition.2019.02.010) is the instance where it worked: a randomised forecast-correcting treatment made forecasts less negative (p = .028) and shifted a real choice of political material, with a preregistered replication of the choice effect. The authors concede the treatment also moved reported experience and that demand may partly explain this, so cite it as the non-discriminating top row in action, not as a discriminator. Do not use its "34%" figure. Add the references in the page's style. Set `ai_modified` from `date -u`. Append your model to `ai_system` only if it is absent anywhere in the string. Write the changelog entry before syncing, run `uv run python scripts/sync.py`, and verify both trees. No "This is not X. It is Y."; no "load-bearing".

### ✓ 2026-10-22: Writing-style guide has no rule for literature absence and exclusivity claims; miswanting's "the one forecast-driven case" and "no design measured felt anticipation" came from author/title-only searches and spread unscoped to two dependent pages
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/project/writing-style.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-10-22, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude] — synthesis at [outer-review-synthesis-2026-10-22](/reviews/outer-review-synthesis-2026-10-22/) cluster 7. Upgraded P2→P1. ChatGPT: "Use “No study meeting criterion X was located in searches Y and Z as of [date]” rather than “no study measured” or “the only case.”" Claude: "Any “only”, “no study” or “single study” sentence must state its inclusion criterion inline, and the metadata must carry the same qualifier." Clause (a) below matches both reviewers. Clause (b), the search that licenses an absence claim, matches ChatGPT's improvement 22 and Claude's "known controversy" query. Clause (c) comes from the processing pass's propagation finding. Keep the 120-word cap. The rule's absence was re-verified at synthesis time: 0 hits for "no study|exhaustive|absence claim|exclusivity", against the positive control "load-bearing" at 8. Precedent: AI refine-drafts added rules to this file on 2026-10-06 (a5d33abc36) and 2026-10-07 (24dc713806). From outer review 2026-10-22 (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro), improvements 22–23, verified on disk 2026-10-22. The guide covers falsifier negatives ("Falsifier Sections: Distinguish the Three Negative States") and source currency ("Citation Aging in Fast-Moving Empirical Fields"). Nothing governs claims that no study did X, or that a study is the only one doing X. A grep for "absence|exhaustive|no study" found nothing, against the positive control "load-bearing" (8 hits). HOW IT FAILED: `research/miswanting-and-forecast-driven-mischoice-2026-10-12` listed only author/title lookups for known studies as its search queries. Its Gaps section correctly said the absence "was not specifically searched". The article then put the absence in its description ("no design measured felt anticipation at choice"), and two pages restated it as "No study has yet measured …". The literature names the construct "immediate emotions" / "integral affect" (Schlösser et al. 2013; Young et al. 2019), and no search used those terms. ADD a short rule (≤ 120 words) under "Evidential Calibration in Articles", after "Citation Aging", along these lines. (a) "No study …" and "the only …" claims about a literature are written scoped to the search ("no study the Map has found …"). (b) Such a claim may appear in a description or lede only if the research pass ran a search on the construct under the field's own vocabulary and synonyms, plus a forward-citation search from the closest hit. Author/title lookups for known studies cannot license an absence. (c) Pages that cite the claim inherit its scope. Give the miswanting case as the worked instance in one sentence. writing-style.md is already long (9,808 by analyze_length), so add nothing else. Set `ai_modified` from `date -u`. Write the changelog entry before syncing, run `uv run python scripts/sync.py`, and verify both trees. No "This is not X. It is Y."; no "load-bearing" in the new text. CROSS-SERVICE CORROBORATION (Claude Fable 5.1, `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-22-claude-fable-5-1.md`, Part 5 bullets 1 and 4; Part 6 items 19 and 22). Claude independently proposes two rules. First, every "only", "no study" or "single study" claim states its inclusion criterion inline, and the metadata carries the same qualifier. Second, a "known controversy" query (meta-analyses, artifact critiques, replications) runs before an empirical construct is drafted. If it fits inside the 120 words, fold the second into clause (b), as part of the search that licenses an absence claim. Claude's further worked instance is miswanting L73, "the one place in this literature". It is false: Loewenstein et al. 2001 name felt affect as a decision input. The miswanting P1 now carries that fix, so use it here only if a second example is wanted.

### ✓ 2026-10-22: Two pages repeat miswanting's absence claim, unscoped: "no study has yet measured the felt anticipation present at choice" — `topics/inheritance-problem-for-the-psychophysical-law` L88 and `concepts/psychophysical-social-choice-problem` L40

### ✓ 2026-10-22: `concepts/miswanting` misses the emotions-as-outcome countermodel (Davis, Love & Maddox 2009); L81 calls miswanting "the demonstrated cost" of a driver whose role L67 says is unknown; L29 calls Gilbert–Wilson wanting "the reverse" of Berridge's
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/miswanting.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-10-22, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude] — synthesis at [outer-review-synthesis-2026-10-22](/reviews/outer-review-synthesis-2026-10-22/) clusters 4–6. Upgraded P2→P1. Three items in this task are convergent. Item (3), L29 "reverse": ChatGPT says "Gilbert–Wilson wanting and Berridge “wanting” are therefore not reversals of one another."; Claude says "The two concepts are orthogonal, not inverse". Item (2), L81 "demonstrated cost": ChatGPT says "That is a framework-internal implication, not a demonstrated cost of an empirically established driver."; Claude grades "miswanting as “the demonstrated cost” of a felt selector" as "*Compatible*, and no more." Item (6), forecasts that leave rankings intact: ChatGPT, "Forecasts can be wrong in magnitude but right for choice"; Claude, "A forecast can be wrong in level yet still *rank* options correctly, and ranking is all that choice needs." Item (6) is therefore REQUIRED, not optional. It needs no new citation: link [affective-forecasting-gap](/concepts/affective-forecasting-gap/), whose L82 carries Moeck et al. and the intensity result. Mathieu & Gosling 2012 is a lead only, because its figures are unchecked. Items (1) countermodel, (4) Berridge citations and (5) L77 are single-reviewer findings that ride in this task. Sequencing is unchanged: run after the miswanting P1, which sits earlier in the file, so FIFO picks it first. The OLD strings at L29, L77 and L81 were re-verified at count 1 at synthesis time. From outer review 2026-10-22 (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro) §§6–8, improvements 11, 12 and 15. Sources were verified against raw text (see Verification Notes). SAME FILE as the 2026-10-22 P1 miswanting task: run after it if both are open, and re-read the file first. LENGTH now 2,346 (concepts hard 3,500, gate `>=`); aim for at most +120 net and re-measure. (1) COUNTERMODEL, the strongest missed objection to [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1): if anticipatory affect is produced by the deciding rather than fed into it, its presence at choice is no sign that it selects. Source: Davis, T., Love, B. C., & Maddox, W. T. (2009). Anticipatory emotions in decision tasks: Covert markers of value or attentional processes? *Cognition*, 112(1), 195–200. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cognition.2009.04.002. It contrasts "emotions-as-input" with "emotions-as-outcome" and reports "We find that emotions are associated with responses as opposed to stimuli" (anticipatory skin-conductance responses). LIMITS TO STATE: the task was rule-plus-exception category learning ("there is no economic valence in a rule-plus-exception task"), so the contrast is stimulus risk versus response, not value. The authors call it "not a refutation of the theory as a whole". It measured arousal, not felt valence. For the timing trap, Grimani et al. 2024 (*Psychological Research* 88(8), 2234–2250, https://doi.org/10.1007/s00426-024-02001-3) is a concrete case: its "anticipatory" item ("How do you feel right now about choosing the sure option?") was asked after each choice. Place this in the "They do not measure the felt input" paragraph (L67) as a further reason existing measures cannot carry [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1), unless the P1 task has restructured that paragraph. Apply the direct-refutation discipline. Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** The classification is editor-internal; it belongs in the refine-draft / deep-review changelog entry, not in the article. If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. (2) L81 OLD: "miswanting is the demonstrated cost of that driver." L67 says whether the felt vehicle carried the misranking is unknown, so inside the conditional this is a cost the driver would inherit, not one shown. Reword it, e.g. "miswanting is a cost that driver would inherit". Tenet 3 claims should state fit without claiming evidence. (3) L29 OLD: "the reverse of Berridge's incentive-salience "wanting", which can decouple from predictions of outcome value altogether." The shared terminology map (`topics/wanting-liking-and-the-value-in-mechanism-fork` L93) blocks equating "wanting" with predicted utility. Gilbert–Wilson wanting IS predicted utility, so the two are different constructs, not inversions; reword along the lines of "a different construct from". If a cite is wanted, Berridge & Robinson 2016 (*American Psychologist* 71(8), 670–679, https://doi.org/10.1037/amp0000059) distinguish incentive salience from "a cognitive desire with a declarative goal". Do not cite that paper for "liking can be nonconscious": it does not say so. REJECTED by the processing pass, so do not do these: rewording "the rival predicts every reversal" (Stability Notes), expanding the welfare or revealed-preference section (Stability Notes), moving the Many-Worlds paragraph (Stability Notes). Set `ai_modified` from `date -u`. Append your model to `ai_system` only if it is absent anywhere in the string. Write the changelog entry before syncing, run `uv run python scripts/sync.py`, and verify both trees. No "This is not X. It is Y."; no "load-bearing". CROSS-SERVICE CORROBORATION (Claude Fable 5.1, `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-22-claude-fable-5-1.md`, Part 2.4): Claude independently flags item (3). It calls Gilbert–Wilson wanting and Berridge's incentive salience "orthogonal, not inverse", and notes that no Berridge paper is cited. ADDITIONS FROM THE CLAUDE REVIEW, verified 2026-10-22: (4) Cite Berridge, K. C. (2023). Separating desire from prediction of outcome value. *Trends in Cognitive Sciences*, 27(10), 932–946. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tics.2023.07.007. It sources L29 directly; the abstract says "desire can decouple completely from memories and from learned predictions of outcome value". It also attributes L77's unsourced "hunger also raises incentive salience". The full text (PMC10527990) says "cue-triggered ‘wanting’ intensity is dynamically modulated in amplitude and valence by changes in relevant brain and physiological states, such as hunger, satiety, drug intoxication, stress". That sentence is in the full text, not the abstract. (5) L77 OLD: "Misattribution describes a cost [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1) accepts rather than evidence against it." The reviewer is right that, as worded, no misattribution result could count against [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1). State the prediction and its defeater instead. A felt currency should be contaminated by incidental feeling, so misattribution fits [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1) without evidencing it; the rival has the incentive-salience analogue. The defeater: choices left unmoved by induced incidental affect, with learned value and bodily state held fixed, would count against [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1). Keep this to about +25 words. Do not concede that the feeling is idle. (6) REQUIRED (convergent; see the synthesis prefix): one clause of about +15 words, at L69 ("Their standards are relative") or at L31. It should say that most documented forecast error is absolute and leaves rankings intact, and link [affective-forecasting-gap](/concepts/affective-forecasting-gap/) L82, which already makes the argument and links back here. This supports the "rarely" thesis. Two reviewers converge on it: ChatGPT §7 ("Forecasts can be wrong in magnitude but right for choice"), which the ChatGPT pass did not task, and Claude Part 2.3. REJECTED, do not do: (i) deleting the Many-Worlds paragraph; (ii) expanding revealed preference; (iii) calling the L83 experiment "ill-posed" on active inference. The first two are covered by the 2026-10-15 deep review's Stability Notes; the review file's Verification Notes give the reasons for the third. BUDGET: now about +170 net.

### ✓ 2026-10-22: `concepts/miswanting` description, lede (L31), L37, L47 heading and L67 claim Hsee & Zhang is the only forecast-driven case and no design measured felt anticipation at choice; Dorison et al. 2019 and Schlösser et al. 2013 / Young et al. 2019 contradict both
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/miswanting.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-10-22, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude] — synthesis at [outer-review-synthesis-2026-10-22](/reviews/outer-review-synthesis-2026-10-22/) clusters 1–3. Already P1 (the ceiling), so not upgraded. The two reviewers reach the same verdict from DISJOINT evidence: ChatGPT cites Dorison 2019, Schlösser 2013 and Young 2019; Claude cites Mellers 1999, Wilson 1993 and Shiv & Huber 2000. Both find the description, L31 and L37 false as worded. ChatGPT: "The claim that nobody measured felt anticipation at choice is false." Claude: "That directly falsifies the article’s sentence “Only Hsee and Zhang measured forecasts, choices and experiences together”". Both also leave the same residue standing, and this task keeps it. That residue is: Study 3 has no independent replication, and no located design dissociates felt anticipation at choice from forecast content and learned value and sets it against later experience (L83). Claude's share of cluster 2 rests on its scoping verdict (the metadata "drops even the “alongside later experience” qualifier"). It does NOT rest on Bechara or Knutson, which are not felt-state reports; see REJECTED below. Re-verified at synthesis time: miswanting.md is unchanged since 2026-10-15 (2,346 words), and every OLD string below is present at count 1. The rest of these notes come unchanged from the two per-review passes. From outer review 2026-10-22 (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro) §§1–3 and improvements 1–6. Every source fact below was verified against raw text on 2026-10-22 (see the review file's Verification Notes). Cite only these facts, not the reviewer's paraphrases. LENGTH: analyze_length 2,346 (concepts soft 2,500, hard 3,500, gate `>=`). Budget about +250 net; the soft band has no consequence. Re-measure before and after. DEFECTS (OLD strings, each unique, verified 04:00Z): (1) L3 description "forecast-driven mischoice rests on one unreplicated study, and no design measured felt anticipation at choice." Both halves are wrong as worded: rewrite it scoped, and say "no independent replication" rather than "unreplicated". (2) L31 "The only reversal shown to be carried by a forecast, Hsee and Zhang's (2004) Study 3, has no independent replication on record" and "No study found for this article measured the felt anticipation present at choice". (3) L37 "Only Hsee and Zhang measured forecasts, choices and experiences together and showed forecasts, from a separate group, matching the choices." and "No study manipulated a forecast while holding the other inputs fixed, the dissociation the test battery in [affective-forecasting-gap](/concepts/affective-forecasting-gap/) needs, so the forecast's causal role is everywhere inferred." (4) L47 heading "### Comparison Mode: The One Forecast-Driven Case". No page links to a miswanting anchor, so renaming is safe. (5) L67 "No study found measured the felt state at choice alongside later experience, so whether the felt vehicle carried the same misranking is unknown." VERIFIED LITERATURE: (a) Dorison, C. A., Minson, J. A., & Rogers, T. (2019). Selective exposure partly relies on faulty affective forecasts. *Cognition*, 188, 98–107. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cognition.2019.02.010. Five studies, N = 2,455. In Study 4, people forecast opposing-party political speech as worse than they then experienced it (M = −1.98 vs −0.93). A randomised treatment made forecasts less negative (−1.77 vs −2.20, p = .028) and cut selective exposure by 24% in a real choice of which senators' press pages to view. In Study 5, participants made a real choice between two videos they then watched, and the intervention reduced own-party choice. Studies 2, 3 and 5 were preregistered, as was a replication of Study 4 (N = 1,205). CAVEATS TO STATE: the Study 4 treatment also made reported experience less negative (−0.59 vs −1.29), and the authors concede demand may partly explain it, so the forecast *error* was not shown to shrink. Study 5 and the replication measured no forecasts. Nobody rated the material they chose. Selective exposure is not a hedonic rank reversal, since people may value the information. Do NOT use the "34%" figure: the paper's own numbers conflict. RESULT: a forecast-targeting randomised intervention has changed real choices, so "everywhere inferred" and "the one forecast-driven case" are false. What survives is that Hsee & Zhang Study 3 is the only located case pairing forecast–choice correspondence with a reversed experienced ranking, and that no intervention isolated the forecast from beliefs, present affect and learned value (Dorison's also moved experience). (b) Schlösser, T., Dunning, D., & Fetchenhauer, D. (2013). What a feeling: The role of immediate and anticipated emotions in risky decisions. *Journal of Behavioral Decision Making*, 26(1), 13–30. https://doi.org/10.1002/bdm.757. The abstract is verbatim-verified: "Immediate emotions refer to the 'hot' visceral feelings people feel as they contemplate a specific decision option at the cusp of making a decision." It reports four studies and says "Decisions were substantively predicted by immediate emotional states beyond anticipated emotions or the subjective probability attached to outcomes." The published full text was NOT reachable, so describe the paper at abstract level only. Do NOT carry the reviewer's "Study 4, 108 participants, post-outcome emotion" details. They are verified only in Schlösser's 2009 Köln dissertation, where post-outcome feeling was compared with ANTICIPATED feeling, forecasts were "strikingly accurate", and the immediate-emotion increment was marginal (p < .081). (c) Young, N. A., Shuster, M. M., & Mikels, J. A. (2019). The sure thing: The role of integral affect in risky choice framing. *Emotion*, 19(6), 1035–1043. https://doi.org/10.1037/emo0000505. Immediate-affect probes ("How do you feel about the choice on the left?") were "presented prior to participants' decision". Immediate affect toward the sure option partially mediated the framing effect. The one anticipated-affect mediator tested (Experiment 2) was null. N = 32 and 24, with no experienced-outcome measure. WHAT TO WRITE: felt anticipation at choice HAS been measured, as option-directed immediate emotion before real choices, and it predicts choice beyond anticipated emotion. What no study the Map has found does is pair that measure with a misranking against later experience, or dissociate it from forecast content and learned value. L83's "What would move the question is the measurement no study here made: felt anticipation at choice, dissociated from forecast content and learned value, set against later experience." stays true, so keep it. CALIBRATION: these results FIT [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1)'s candidate currency without evidencing it: the affect was measured not manipulated, mediation is not causal identification, and the value-blind rival reads immediate affect as a correlate of learned value. Say so, but do not concede that the feeling is idle (never concede epiphenomenalism). Do NOT set aside present-tense reports of one's own feeling as "cool judgements". L67's sentence about Hsee & Zhang's predictions of OTHERS' happiness is correct as it stands. Scope every absence claim to the search ("no study the Map has found …"), including in the description. OPTIONAL (+~15 words): note that Hsee & Zhang's own audiovisual replication exists only as an unpublished 2003 manuscript (research note L96), which is why "independent" matters. Add the three references in the article's numbered style. DEPENDENTS: two pages repeat the unscoped claim, and a separate P2 task covers them, so do not edit them here. Set `ai_modified` from `date -u`. Append the model you actually run as to `ai_system` only if it is absent anywhere in the string (now `claude-opus-5-5+claude-fable-5-1`). Write the changelog entry before syncing, run `uv run python scripts/sync.py`, and verify the new wording in BOTH the obsidian and hugo trees. No "This is not X. It is Y."; no "load-bearing". CROSS-SERVICE CORROBORATION (Claude Fable 5.1, `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-22-claude-fable-5-1.md`, Parts 2.3 and 3, Claims A and B): Claude independently finds three things. The description's "rests on one unreplicated study" and "no design measured felt anticipation at choice" are false as worded. L37's "Only Hsee and Zhang measured forecasts, choices and experiences together" is false. L31's narrow claim, that Study 3 has no independent replication, is true. ADDITIONS FROM THE CLAUDE REVIEW, verified 2026-10-22 ~04:30Z at Crossref plus the abstract or full text. Register them, so the rewrite does not install a new exclusivity claim. (d) Wilson, T. D., Lisle, D. J., Schooler, J. W., Hodges, S. D., Klaaren, K. J., & LaFleur, S. J. (1993). Introspecting about reasons can reduce post-choice satisfaction. *Personality and Social Psychology Bulletin*, 19(3), 331–339. https://doi.org/10.1177/0146167293193010. It is reprinted in the same Lichtenstein & Slovic 2006 volume as ref 1, pp. 471–486. Abstract, verbatim: "Those instructed to think about their reasons chose a different type of poster than control subjects and, when contacted 3 weeks later, were less satisfied with their choice." This is a RANDOMISED manipulation of the evaluation that grounds wanting, with a real choice and later satisfaction measured. It is the ladder rung L37 omits. Caveats to state: (i) it is between-subjects; the reviewer's "within-person" is wrong; (ii) no forecast was measured, so the mechanism is reasons-induced attitude change; (iii) the standard is later satisfaction relative to controls; (iv) it is verified at abstract level only, so give no N or effect sizes. It fits the "rarely, and mostly not through forecasting" thesis. Write it as the randomised case of a changed choice followed by lower satisfaction, which runs through reasons analysis rather than a measured forecast. (e) Mellers, B. A., Schwartz, A., & Ritov, I. (1999). Emotion-based choice. *Journal of Experimental Psychology: General*, 128(3), 332–345. https://doi.org/10.1037/0096-3445.128.3.332. Use it as summarised in Mellers, B. A., & McGraw, A. P. (2001). Anticipated emotions as guides to choice. *Current Directions in Psychological Science*, 10(6), 210–214. https://doi.org/10.1111/1467-8721.00151 (full text read). The gamble paradigm measured three things: choices; anticipated pleasure (hypothetical-outcome conditions); and actual pleasure (real-outcome conditions). Choices predicted from anticipated pleasure "were correlated with actual choices in five different experiments" ("ranged from .66 to .86, with an average of .74"). "In the laboratory studies, predictions were quite accurate." So a design that measured all three found NO misranking. That falsifies L37's "Only Hsee and Zhang measured forecasts, choices and experiences together" and supports the "rarely" thesis. Do NOT carry two of the reviewer's claims. First, its ".33 once SEU removed" is not a fall: it is the residual correlation, which shows anticipated pleasure adding predictability over utilities. Second, its claim that participants anticipated feelings after choosing and before the outcome is unverified. The pregnancy and dieting overestimates of displeasure involved no choice. (f) NEW OLD STRING in the same defect class: L73 "The third is the one place in this literature where the forecast's *felt* vehicle is named as an input." It is false as worded. See Loewenstein, G. F., Weber, E. U., Hsee, C. K., & Welch, N. (2001). Risk as feelings. *Psychological Bulletin*, 127(2), 267–286. https://doi.org/10.1037/0033-2909.127.2.267. Its abstract says the risk-as-feelings hypothesis "highlights the role of affect experienced at the moment of decision making" and finds that "emotional reactions often drive behavior". Scope the sentence to Gilbert and Wilson's account, e.g. "The third is where Gilbert and Wilson name the forecast's *felt* vehicle as an input". If words allow, note risk-as-feelings as its decision-literature counterpart. Loewenstein et al. are naturalists, so cite them for the input claim only. OPTIONAL, measure first: Shiv, B., & Huber, J. (2000). The impact of anticipating satisfaction on consumer choice. *Journal of Consumer Research*, 27(2), 202–216. https://doi.org/10.1086/314320. Preferences "both expressed and revealed" shift when anticipated satisfaction is evoked, via mental imagery. There is no experienced-outcome measure (0 hits for "experienced" in the full text), so it is a forecast-mode half-chain like Dorison's. REJECTED from the Claude review for this task: its readings of Bechara et al. 1997 and Knutson et al. 2007 as rival-favouring, and as grounds for lowering [P-VS1](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs1). Knutson's own abstract reads the neural signals as "circuits related to anticipatory affect" that "precedes and supports" purchasing decisions. Bechara's before-knowing claim is contested by Maia & McClelland 2004, already cited in `concepts/somatic-interface`. Neither study is a felt-state report, so neither bears on Claim B as this task scopes it. BUDGET: these additions raise the net to about +320 words. Re-measure against the hard limit of 3,500.

FINDING: 'protective' needs a timescale. - Long run: peritraumatic dissociation predicts WORSE outcomes (Ozer 2003 r=.35; Breh & Seidler 2007 .36; Lensvelt-Mulders 2008 .401, 59 studies).; - Most of that predictive power vanishes once persistent dissociation and early symptoms are controlled (Werner & Griffin 2012 keep a unique 4%).; - At the moment of trauma, the one direct test of momentary distress relief (Fikretoglu 2006, N=1,026) is null; panic explains 47%. Griffin 1997: high dissociators reported MORE distress with suppressed arousal.; - Measurement is retrospective and drifts with current symptoms (Thompson-Hollands 2021).; - The failure lands EQUALLY on the interface reading and the rival's defence cascade. The subtype page's verdict stands, neither side gains, and Dualism stays at 'compatible with, not evidence for'. WARNING: Fikretoglu uses the word 'epiphenomenon'. Do not let it read as the Map conceding epiphenomenalism. Ozer 2003, Briere 2005 and Marmar 1994 are abstract-only; the Marmar 1997 PDEQ chapter and Bryant 2009/2010 are unread (do-not-characterise). Do not cite Tortella-Feliu 2019 for a grade. Slug `peritraumatic-dissociation`; target 2,500-2,800 words (about 600 of them references), under topics soft 3,000. ### ✓ 2026-10-22: Research Peritraumatic dissociation and the protective reading
- **Type**: research-topic
- **Notes**: Harvested from the review corpus (optimistic-2026-10-21-scorekeeping-wing.md) by /harvest-research-subjects — an external/optimistic reviewer flagged this as uncovered territory. optimistic-2026-10-21-scorekeeping-wing (High Priority, NOT COVERED). The flagship clinical-dissociation-as-systematic-evidence and concepts/dissociative-subtype-of-ptsd (L37, L92) take as their datum that trauma-linked dissociation is 'selective and protective', but the predictive literature tests 'protective' directly and goes unmentioned. Ozer, Best, Lipsey & Weiss 2003 (Psych Bull 129(1) 52-73) find peritraumatic dissociation 'the largest (weighted r = .35)' of seven PTSD predictors. Briere, Scott & Weathers 2005 (AJP 162(12) 2295-2301) find it no longer predicts 'once other variables (especially persistent and generalized dissociation) were taken into account'. Lensvelt-Mulders et al. 2008 is a meta-analytic lead, metadata only. So 'protective' needs a timescale, and measurement is mostly retrospective self-report, which ties into the Map's introspection voids. Driver re-measured 01:38Z: 'peritraumatic' 0 live / 1 research (a table row in research/dissociative-subtype-of-ptsd-2026-10-09 L95, Griffin et al. 1997) / 0 archive; 'Briere' 0/0/0; 'Marmar' 0/0/0; positive control 'PTSD' 5 live / 11 research. The 2 live 'Ozer' hits are unrelated authors. CALIBRATION (binding): the page must not claw back the subtype page's verdict that the trauma point goes to the rival; if the momentary-protection reading fails, it fails for both the interface reading and the rival's defence cascade, and neither gains. Dualism stays at 'compatible with, not evidence for'. Leads to VERIFY at source: Ozer 2003, Briere 2005, Lensvelt-Mulders 2008, Marmar's Peritraumatic Dissociative Experiences Questionnaire, Griffin et al. 1997. Topics (356/370) preferred; concepts is tight (362/370). Target section: topics (cap headroom confirmed at harvest). Output a research note to obsidian/research/peritraumatic-dissociation-and-the-protective-reading-2026-10-22.md; the research→expand-topic chain may then create a new topics/ article. CALIBRATION: this is a methods subject, neutral between dualism and physicalism. 'Content' in Ericsson & Simon means information heeded in short-term memory, not phenomenal content, so their results cannot be used against the Schwitzgebel caveat or to vindicate introspection of phenomenal character. Ericsson & Simon relocate the CAUSE of Nisbett & Wilson's failures to procedure (delayed, general and 'why' probes); they do not move the content/process line. Subjects report what they attended to and the analyst infers the process. Level 3 (1980) covers filtering and inference as well as explanation, so it is broader than 'requests for explanation'. 'Reactive' does not mean worse: explanation instructions raised accuracy. Ericsson & Simon did not invent thinking aloud, and the origin of the term 'protocol analysis' is unsettled: credit no coiner. DO-NOT-CHARACTERISE (unread): the 1984/1993 book itself, Wilson 1994, Ericsson 2003, White 1980 and Bowles 2010. Target about 2,200 words (about 1,850 prose); topics hard 4,000. (1) Hempel's dilemma applied to closure: "if 'physical' means what current physics describes, then causal closure looks false … If 'physical' means what a future completed physics describes, the claim looks empty". (2) A verdict: "On Montero's analysis, no available definition of 'physical' supports causal closure without either circularity or vacuity." Montero 2003, newdualism.org copy (fetch with a browser User-Agent and Accept header; a bare curl gets a mod_security 406), grepped verbatim, contradicts (2). Footnote 25: "As I see it, this dilemma, alone, is not fatal to definitions of the physical in terms of physics." She also offers "the fundamentally nonmental" as "the only useful notion of the physical", and she discusses an argument for "the causal closure of the fundamentally nonmental" stated in terms of current physiology, which she says can avoid Hempel's dilemma. Her horns are "a theory we cannot defend" and "impossibly vague"; her one "vacuous" concerns quantum events lacking sufficient causes. The closure application in (1) is closer to Gillett and Witmer (2001) as she reports them ("a similar problem arises for the physiological argument for physicalism") than to Montero's own view. Rewrite (2) to what she says, and attribute or scope (1). Keep the first sentence's credit for distinguishing several closure principles, which is accurate (CC, SCC, TCC). LENGTH: 3463 by analyze_length against topics hard 4000. Do not widen into a Hempel survey: the P3 research task hempels-dilemma-and-the-definition-of-the-physical covers the literature.

L108: "If it means what a completed future physics describes, the claim is vacuous since we cannot know whether that physics will include or exclude mental causation (Hempel's dilemma). So (1) replace "vacuous" with her "impossibly vague" or mark the closure application as the Map's own; (2) the closing "unfalsifiable in principle" runs the epistemic charge together with the semantic one, so pick one; (3) "closure is false" on the first horn overstates her "cannot defend" and "most likely, neither a true nor complete account", so prefer "probably false". The intrinsic-nature wording to stay consistent with: "the claim is, in Montero's phrase, 'impossibly vague' (Montero 2003): the charge is not that it cannot be tested but that its content waits on a physics no one can yet specify". L61: "no physics—present or completed—delivers intrinsic natures by the structural method". L65: "in principle, hidden". Make each conditional on the structuralist reading of physics, as L43 is. (b) **The bearer premise.** L55 gives the quidditist's "relations require relata, dispositions require bearers, a role requires an occupant", and the Map sides with it. The page never states the pure-powers reply, that a disposition can be its own bearer and so needs no categorical occupant: 0 hits for pure power, pandisposition, Bird or Mumford. Nor does it say that powerful qualities deny the occupant/role split rather than supply an occupant. The Map already treats both, at consciousness-and-the-metaphysics-of-laws-and-dispositions L62 (pandispositionalism, Bird, Shoemaker) and its section "The Pandispositionalist Challenge" (L106), and at concepts/causal-powers. Add a short paragraph that states the reply and the Map's answer, links those pages, and says what the Map's siding with the quidditist rests on. Also add one sentence on SEP's caution (§1.1) that the structural/non-structural distinction is not the relational/intrinsic one: a ball's sphericality is non-relational. That caution is why the literature uses Pereboom's relatively/absolutely intrinsic distinction, so the L47–L53 lists should say which they mean. (c) **Falsifier (1)** at L93 asks for an OSR showing "relations require no relata and structure no occupant". L55 already grants that moderate OSR (Esfeld and Lam 2008) keeps relata and still denies quiddities. ontic-structural-realism L45 says an argument aimed at relata-free relations "refutes the version nobody needs to hold". Retarget the falsifier at a structuralism that accounts for individuation, modality and causation without independently individuated categorical natures. Carry across that page's L83 point that the epistemic reading is left "owing an account of its own individuator that the Map does not have"; L85 here imports only the asymmetry. (d) **Hempel and closure (L59–L61).** "The intrinsic-nature distinction explains *why* the dilemma has force" and the uncited closure sentence ("closure stated over current physics is false, and stated over completed physics is vacuous") go past the source. Montero 2003 (newdualism.org copy) gives the horns as "cannot defend" and "impossibly vague", and separates CC, strong closure (SCC) and two-way closure. Her one use of "vacuous" concerns quantum events that lack sufficient causes, not completed physics. Scope L61: Hempel's dilemma is a problem for defining "physical" by reference to physics, and gives no independent evidence that physics omits quiddities. Either cite the closure claim or mark it as the Map's own extrapolation. (e) **Identity versus selection, and what "at the intrinsic base" means.** L83 says that if consciousness were the intrinsic nature of physical entities it "could not causally *select* among physical outcomes". consciousness-and-the-metaphysics-of-laws-and-dispositions L158 has the powerful quality's identity of quality and power do the selecting: "the felt quality present at the site selects its own manifestation". L87 here reads the intrinsic base as "causally contributing". Neither page states the distinction that reconciles them: identity within a phenomenal powerful quality, versus identity of consciousness with physical quiddities. L37 and L81 also place the non-physical aspect "at the intrinsic base" yet distinct from quiddities, without saying what "at" means: the realiser of physical dispositions, the level of fundamental ontology, or the site of a psychophysical relation. Add one or two sentences at L83 that draw the distinction and fix the sense of "at". Also make clear that the selection objection is internal to the Map's Tenets 2–4 model. It shows that Russellian identity is incompatible with that model, not that identity theories lack causal efficacy. Apply the direct-refutation discipline. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. (a) is its Minor 3, the same-paragraph assert-and-hedge at L43. (b) is its M1–M3: the bearer premise against dispositional monism and powerful qualities, plus the relatively/absolutely intrinsic distinction. (d) is its M4, Hempel. (e) is its M5 and M8, identity versus selection and what "at the intrinsic base" means. Claude rates (e) its single most serious finding. L37 and L81 here place the non-physical aspect "at the intrinsic base", but bi-aspectual-ontology L37 says "Consciousness supplies that actuality where a neural interface exists; elsewhere physical objective reduction fixes outcomes without it". The intrinsic base is everywhere, while that actuality is local, so the sense of "at" chosen in (e) must be consistent with that sentence.
