Four additions follow, each verified on disk or at source.
  (f) **Hempel wording.** L59 calls the second horn "unfalsifiable", and L61 calls closure over completed physics "vacuous". The first charge is epistemic and the second semantic. Pick one, consistent with (d)'s reading of Montero ("impossibly vague").
  (g) **Quiddities may be physical.** L61's "Neither horn touches quiddities" assumes quiddities are non-physical. Russellian physicalists deny that. Pereboom, "Consciousness, Physicalism, and Absolutely Intrinsic Properties" (Alter & Nagasawa eds. 2015, pp. 300–323, checked against his preprint) argues that absolutely intrinsic categorical properties may be physical. Wilson's no-fundamental-mentality constraint ("On characterizing the physical", *Phil. Studies* 131(1) 61–99, 2006, Crossref-verified) defines "physical" so as to admit non-mental quiddities. Name this reply in one clause and scope L61 to readings that are not Russellian-physicalist. If (b) uses the relatively/absolutely intrinsic distinction, cite this Pereboom chapter for it.
  (h) **Falsifiers.** Falsifier (2) at L93 cannot be met on the article's own terms, because the article defines the method of physics as structural. Restate it as something that could happen, for example a successful anti-quidditist account of fundamental properties. Falsifier (3) holds Russellian monism to a no-combination-problem standard the Map does not meet itself. ### ✓ 2026-10-21: `concepts/intrinsic-nature` L41 quotes a Russell sentence as from *The Analysis of Matter*, which does not contain it; also fix the Pautz 2015 container in ref 6 and the Lewis credit at L35
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/intrinsic-nature.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-10-21, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude]. Already P1, so not raised; rewritten by /combine-outer-reviews 2026-10-21 (see `reviews/outer-review-synthesis-2026-10-21.md` cluster 1). Both reviewers flag all four citation defects. On Russell, ChatGPT says the sentence "does not appear to be a verbatim sentence from *The Analysis of Matter*" (§9.1) and Claude says "The sentence is in *Human Knowledge: Its Scope and Limits* (1948)" (row 1). Both also flag the Lewis credit (ChatGPT §9.2, "Lewis is miscredited"; Claude row 3), the Pautz 2015 container (ChatGPT §9.3; Claude row 8) and the missing Hempel source (ChatGPT §9.4, "Hempel's dilemma lacks its central sources"; Claude row 6, "Hempel's original is not cited"). The synthesis made three changes. (i) Russell was re-verified independently at about 05:00Z. Gutenberg 77427 (*The Analysis of Matter*) has 0 hits for "only known as regards", against 1 hit for the positive control "exceedingly abstract". The raw archive.org djvu text of *Human Knowledge* has 1 hit, followed by "—features which, because of their abstractness, do not suffice to show…". The sentence appears nowhere in obsidian/ or archive/ except this page, and nowhere in hugo/content except its Hugo copy. (ii) Item (4) below is convergent and is no longer optional. Add one primary source for Hempel's dilemma, verified at the publisher first. If verification fails, say so in the report rather than adding an unverified reference. (iii) Optional item (7): Eddington's "The external world of physics has thus become a world of shadows" is on p. xvi of the Introduction (gutenberg.ca HTML, anchor `Page_xvi`, checked at synthesis). ChatGPT §9.5 asked for a page, and Claude row 2 supplied it. A page number at L41 or on ref 2 costs about +2 words. Original notes follow. From outer review 2026-10-21 (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro §9.1–9.3), verified on disk and at source. Same file as the argument-calibration task below, which this synthesis raised to P1; whichever runs second re-reads the file first. (1) L41 reads: "The physical world is only known as regards certain abstract features of its space-time structure," Russell wrote. The sentence sits under *The Analysis of Matter* and ref 1 (Russell 1927). The Project Gutenberg full text of that book (ebook 77427, the 1927 Kegan Paul / Harcourt first edition, 408 pages) has 0 hits for "only known as regards" and 0 for "abstract features". Strawson (2006) "Realistic Monism" quotes the full sentence and cites it to Russell's *Human Knowledge: Its Scope and Limits* (1948: 240), while saying Russell made the point "in 1927". That is the probable source of the misattribution. The reviewer's guess that the sentence compresses several passages is wrong: it is verbatim, from a different book. Preferred fix, which keeps ref 1: replace the quote with the verbatim *Analysis of Matter* passage on p. 10 (ch. I): "Physics, in itself, is exceedingly abstract, and reveals only certain mathematical characteristics of the material with which it deals. It does not tell us anything as to the intrinsic character of this material." Alternative: keep the 1948 sentence, re-attribute it to *Human Knowledge* and add that reference, but only after checking p. 240 in a raw copy of *Human Knowledge*. Strawson's citation is a lead, not a verification. The 2026-07-19 deep review passed this quote against "the standard-quoted form" in secondary literature, which is how it survived; do not repeat that check. (2) Ref 6 (L117) places Pautz 2015, "A Dilemma for Russellian Monists about Consciousness", in T. Alter & Y. Nagasawa (Eds.), *Consciousness in the Physical World*. The volume's table of contents (Google Books and PhilPapers listings) has no Pautz chapter. Pautz's own site lists the paper as "(2015)" with no venue. Five live sibling pages give "Unpublished manuscript (PhilPapers)": russellian-monism L175, russellian-monism-versus-bi-aspectual-dualism L180, consciousness-and-the-metaphysics-of-laws-and-dispositions L211, substance-property-dualism L202 and disguised-property-dualism L127. Align ref 6 with them. The 2026-06-25 deep review introduced the volume container; do not propagate it anywhere. (3) L35 says the term quiddities is "standardly credited to Lewis (2009)". SEP's *Russellian Monism* entry only cites him: "often called quiddities (Lewis 2009; Chalmers 2012)". Black, R. (2000), "Against Quidditism", *Australasian Journal of Philosophy* 78(1), 87–104, doi 10.1080/00048400012349371 (confirmed at Crossref), used the terminology nine years earlier. Recast the clause so Lewis is a standard source rather than the originator (for example "current in the contemporary debate since Black (2000) and Lewis (2009)"), and add Black 2000 to References. Keep the 2026-09-05 split that credits the distinction to Russell and Eddington and the term to later writers. (4) Optional, and only after verifying at the publisher: the Hempel section (L59) cites no source for the dilemma itself. Hempel (1980) and Crane & Mellor (1990) are the usual sources. Length: 2456 by analyze_length against concepts hard 3500 (1043 headroom); keep this pass near length-neutral. Do not re-run the 2026-06-25 metadata ledger for refs 2–5 and 7–10.
  **Cross-service corroboration 2026-10-21 (Claude Opus 5.5, `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-21-claude-opus-5-5.md`: TL;DR; Citation Verification rows 1, 3 and 8; intrinsic-nature recommendations 1–3).** Claude independently flags all three defects. Processing at about 04:20Z added two verifications. **Russell (1).** The L41 sentence is VERBATIM in the raw archive.org djvu text of *Human Knowledge: Its Scope and Limits* (item `bertrand-russell-human-knowledge-its-scope-and-limits`, 1948), with exactly 1 hit for "only known as regards". It opens Part III, Chapter VII, "Mind and Matter". The copy's page marker 239 falls just before the chapter heading, which puts the sentence on p. 240 and matches Strawson. That meets the "check p. 240 in a raw copy" condition above, so the re-attribution alternative is now open as well as the preferred *Analysis of Matter* p. 10 swap. If you re-attribute, add "Russell, B. (1948). *Human Knowledge: Its Scope and Limits*. George Allen & Unwin" and keep ref 1 for the 1927 thesis. **Lewis credit (3).** SEP *Haecceitism* §7.1 (raw HTML) says "Armstrong (1989: 59) seems to coin the term 'quidditism.'" Its bibliography gives Armstrong, D. M. (1989), *A Combinatorial Theory of Possibility*, Cambridge University Press. Credit the *-ism* to Armstrong and its first sharp statement to Black (2000), and keep Lewis (2009) as a standard source. Do NOT write that Armstrong coined "quiddity". The word is scholastic (*quidditas*), and Claude's recommendation 2 overstates SEP on this point. Two small additions from Claude, both verified on disk. (5) L35 says "The distinction is Bertrand Russell's (1927) and Arthur Eddington's (1928)", but ref 3 is Langton's *Kantian Humility*, which concerns Kant's version of the same distinction. Add "in its modern form" or a three-word nod to Kant, and keep the 09-05 split. (6) Ref 11 cites the SEP "Russellian Monism" entry as a bare URL. Name its authors (Alter and Pereboom) and the revision consulted. Keep this pass near length-neutral.

### ✓ 2026-10-20: cetacean-and-corvid-consciousness — bird-pallium "radically different substrate" framing predates the 2020–2025 circuit-convergence findings, and Gutfreund's own target crow study (Nieder 2020) is never named
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/cetacean-and-corvid-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: From the literature-drift audit 2026-10-20 (one WebSearch spent; every source below verified at Crossref plus a raw PubMed/PMC text grep, never by a WebFetch prompt naming the phrase). Median empirical-citation year is **2008.5** (22 refs, excluding James 1890 and Map self-cites), 17.5 years behind `ai_modified` 2026-08-02. That lag mostly reflects canonical founding experiments (Reiss & Marino 2001, Hunt 1996, Prior 2008), which are appropriate here and are NOT the defect. The defect is two specific claims the 2020–2025 literature changes. Superlative check is clean: "the largest known multilevel alliance network outside humans" matches Connor et al. 2022's abstract ("the largest nonhuman alliance network known", PMID 36037370), and nothing surfaced supersedes it. **Length headroom: 137 words** (`analyze_length` 3862 against topics hard 4000, gate `>=`, so 3999 max). Split: prose ~3086, Further Reading ~105, References ~675. The reference apparatus consumes most of the soft-to-hard band, so do NOT route this to `/condense` on the raw total (the 2026-07-29 deep review says the same). Every addition below must be paid for by trims.
  1. **Avian pallium framing (the main finding).** L49 reads "Bird brains lack neocortex entirely, using a pallial structure of nuclear clusters rather than cortical layers". L81 builds the convergence argument on "radically different substrates (nuclear pallial circuits, distinct neocortex, layered cortical columns)". L108 says "given how radically different the substrates are, 'convergent computation' must identify *which* computational properties they share". That holds for gross anatomy but is dated at the circuit level. **Rueda-Alaña et al. (2025)**, "Evolutionary convergence of sensory circuits in the pallium of amniotes", *Science* 387(6735), eadp3411, doi:10.1126/science.adp3411, PMID 39946453 (22 authors, Rueda-Alaña lead, García-Moreno last; print the full list from PubMed, house style lists all authors). It works in chick, gecko and mouse and finds pallial sensory circuits that are "structurally and functionally equivalent" but whose neurons "are generated at varying developmental times and brain regions across species", with "an early developmental divergence in the transcriptomic progression of glutamatergic neurons". The authors conclude this suggests convergence of high-order sensory processing. That one citation carries both halves of the corrected claim: circuit-level equivalence, and independent developmental origin. The companion paper the search surfaced is **Zaremba et al. (2025)**, "Developmental origins and evolution of pallial cell types and structures in birds", *Science* 387(6735), eadp5182, doi:10.1126/science.adp5182, PMID 39946461. It has 16 authors: Zaremba, Fallahshahroudi, Schneider, Schmidt, Sarropoulos, Leushkin, Berki, Van Poucke, Jensen, Senovilla-Ganzo, Hervas-Sotomayor, Trost, Lamanna, Sepp, García-Moreno, Kaessmann. It finds that pallial excitatory neuron types outside the hippocampus "have diverged" from mammalian ones while inhibitory types are shared. Use it instead of Rueda-Alaña only if it fits the budget better. Do NOT overread its "mesopallium … akin to the mammalian claustrum and deep cortical layers" line as consciousness evidence. **Stacho et al. (2020)**, *Science* 369(6511), eabc5534, doi:10.1126/science.abc5534, PMID 32973004 (column-like canonical circuitry across the layer-like nuclear boundaries) is already cited and formatted at `topics/evolution-under-dualism.md` ref list, but it is optional here given the budget. ⚠️ **Species caveat for every one of these papers**: none studied corvids. Stacho's MeSH terms are Columbidae and Strigiformes (pigeons, owls); Rueda-Alaña used chick; Zaremba used chicken. Write "avian pallium", never "corvid pallium". **Direction matters**: independent developmental origin supports the article's "three independent lineages" premise. Circuit-level equivalence, however, hands the materialist convergent-computation reading a concrete answer to L108's "*which* computational properties they share". Say that plainly in the Neural Architecture Challenge section. This finding helps the rival's specification, not the amplification hypothesis.
  2. **Gutfreund's worked target is a corvid study, and the article never names it.** The Standing Agnostic Challenge section (L112) reconstructs Gutfreund (2024) using dolphin MSR, crow tools and scrub-jay caching as examples. But Gutfreund's paper is a critique of two named studies, and the first is **Nieder, A., Wagener, L., & Rinnert, P. (2020). A neural correlate of sensory consciousness in a corvid bird. *Science*, 369(6511), 1626-1629. https://doi.org/10.1126/science.abb1447** (PMID 32973028; also ref 16 of `topics/interface-efficacy-and-the-cognitive-gap.md`). Its finding: single-neuron responses in the crow pallial endbrain during a visual detection task track the birds' reported stimulus presence or absence, which the authors "argue … is an empirical marker of avian consciousness". Gutfreund's reply, verbatim in the PMC full text (PMC11496166): "the crows do not report their subjective experiences, rather they make a behavioral decision". Naming the study makes the section concrete, and it is the single most on-topic empirical result for an article titled corvid consciousness. This was finding 2 of the 2026-07-28 audit on this file; it was lost when that task's Notes were truncated (the parser bug since fixed), and was never executed. Finding 3 of that audit (Stacho) was lost the same way, which is why item 1 above recurs.
  3. **Sibling, about one word, same fix.** `topics/evolution-under-dualism.md` L113 reads "the corvid pallium is built of nuclear clusters rather than cortical layers, yet tracing work finds a column-like, cortex-like canonical circuit running across those nuclear boundaries (Stacho et al., 2020)". Stacho studied pigeons and owls, so change "corvid pallium" to "avian pallium". That sentence also says "(see [cetacean-and-corvid-consciousness](/topics/cetacean-and-corvid-consciousness/))" for the circuit-level convergence this file does not yet state. Item 1 makes that pointer true.
  4. **Carried lead, UNVERIFIED and not part of this audit's verdict.** The `reviews/optimistic-2026-09-27-evolution-and-origins-wing.md` Calibration Concern 7, written from memory, flags L47's "cortical-neuron counts approaching the human range (Roth & Dicke 2005)" and "Cortical-neuron parity". It says that from memory Roth & Dicke's table gives the bottlenose dolphin about half the human count, with parity reported only for the false killer whale. Check Table 1 at the publisher (TICS 9(5), 250-257, doi:10.1016/j.tics.2005.03.005) before changing anything, and if it holds, restate it without adding words. Nothing has queued this lead since 2026-09-27.
  **Constraints**: keep the five-tier calibration exactly as it is. Cetaceans and corvids stay at *realistic possibility, contested* and the amplification hypothesis at *live hypothesis*. Adding citations raises no evidence grade ([evidential-status-discipline](/project/evidential-status-discipline/)). The 2026-07-29 deep-review stability note says not to re-sweep the 23 existing citations; verify only what you add. Leave Prior 2008, the MSR re-framing and the Taylor & Jelbert / Vanhooland corrections untouched. Paraphrase the 2025 findings rather than quoting them, except Gutfreund's sentence above, which is grep-verified.

### ✓ 2026-10-20: `concepts/agency-budget` L38 lets selection "correlate arbitrarily with C" inside "any publicly assemblable ensemble", but an ensemble re-weighted by instruction is assemblable without reading C, so the budget is zero unless the page adds the companion law the born-preserving apex says the Map owes
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/agency-budget.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-10-20 (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro full-site audit, §2.2 and improvement 4). Verified on disk. THE ARGUMENT: L36 states the constraint Σ_C P(o | C, X) · P(C | X) = q(o | X), and L38 binds it "within any publicly assemblable ensemble". Suppose outsiders can prepare different intention mixes π by instruction, recruitment or incentive, none of which reads C, and the conditionals P(o | c, X) do not themselves change with the preparation. Then the equality holds for each such π. A π concentrated on one state c_i gives P(o | c_i, X) = q(o | X) for every i, so the mutual information the budget prices is zero. L87's dependency paragraph names only one escape: C must be "publicly unresolvable in principle" so that it cannot become a context X. That blocks conditioning on C, not re-weighting π. `apex/born-preserving-causal-efficacy` L95–L99 (section "Where a Selection Could Show, If Anywhere: The Conditional Signature") already runs this argument, from the July 2026 ChatGPT 5.5 Pro review. It concludes that exact preservation "needs a companion law over the distribution of conscious reasons" (psychophysical superselection), "a debt the register should carry alongside [P-Q10](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q10)". The debt has not propagated: `positions/quantum-interface` has 0 hits for "superselection", "re-weight" or "admissib", and `concepts/agency-budget` has 0 for "superselection" and "re-weight". FIX: add a second flagged dependency to L87, or to "What the Budget Does Not Establish" (L99). Preservation under re-weighting forces every conditional to Born unless one of two things holds. Either the re-weightings that would unbalance the marginal are physically unachievable (superselection: a law over the distribution of intending), or the conditionals re-tune per context to that context's intention mix (a compensation law that tracks the population). Link the apex section, and make L38's "arbitrarily" conditional on that dependency. COORDINATION: do not settle [P-Q2](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q2)'s quantifier. The result holds under the pooled reading wherever the ensemble can be re-weighted, and a fortiori under the 2026-10-14 "every publicly conditionable context" reading. See the blocked P1 HUMAN DECISION on [P-Q2](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q2), and do not propagate the 10-14 reading here. Do not edit the register, which is 5964 words (critical). LENGTH: analyze_length reads 2612 against concepts hard 3500 (887 headroom). The install should be about 80–120 words; print analyze_length before and after.

### ✓ 2026-10-20: `concepts/retrocausality` L54–L58 presents interpretations as experimental results: delayed-choice experiments do not show that later choices "determine" earlier behaviour, and Price 2012 answers "Maybe", not "follows necessarily"
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/retrocausality.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-10-20 (Claude Opus 5.5 full-site audit, §5 and recommendation (a)6), plus one locus found by the driver. (1) L58 reads "Experiments confirm that measurement choices made *after* a particle passes through a double-slit can determine whether it behaved as wave or particle *earlier*." Two Crossref-verified sources report interference statistics consistent with standard quantum mechanics. They are Jacques, Wu, Grosshans, Treussart, Grangier, Aspect & Roch (2007), Science 315(5814):966–968, doi 10.1126/science.1136303, and Ma, Kofler & Zeilinger (2016), Rev. Mod. Phys. 88:015005. The RMP abstract frames these tests as excluding systems "a priori adapting to the specific experimental situation". Retro-determination is one interpretation of that result. Rewrite: the experiments rule out pre-set behaviour, and a retrocausal reading is one interpretation of what they show. Keep Vedovato et al. (2017) as an extension of the test, not of the interpretation. (2) L54 reads "Retrocausality has serious proponents and empirical grounding in physics." Change "empirical grounding" to a formal motivation (time-symmetric dynamics). (3) L56 reads "As Huw Price argues, if physics is time-symmetric and causal, retrocausality follows necessarily." The cited paper's Crossref title is "Does time-symmetry imply retrocausality? How the quantum world says 'Maybe'?" (SHPMP 43(2):75–83, doi 10.1016/j.shpsb.2011.12.003). State the argument as conditional and restore the subtitle in the reference at L192. (4) L153 reads "The transactional interpretation makes minimal assumptions". That is a simplicity verdict (Tenet 5; tenets L145/L147), and it says nothing about scope: selecting a whole history, including prior neural states, is a large-scope reading. Tenets L69 defines Tenet 2's minimality as empirical-constraint minimality, so say the reading must satisfy those constraints instead of claiming minimal assumptions. Do not change L81's "require less interpretational commitment". That line concedes the rivals are cheaper and is held behind the blocked Tenet-5 tiebreaker doctrine record. LENGTH: analyze_length reads 3171 against the concepts hard ceiling of 3500, so there are 328 words of headroom. That is comfortable, but keep net additions under about 120 words. Sync to hugo and confirm.

### ✓ 2026-10-20: `topics/testing-consciousness-collapse` L175 says Proietti et al. (2019) "confirmed empirically" that universal quantum mechanics plus definite conscious experience yields contradictions, but the paper tests Brukner's no-go with photon "observers" and its conclusion is conditional
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/testing-consciousness-collapse.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-10-20 (Claude Opus 5.5 full-site audit, §5, §6 and recommendation (a)4). Verified against the paper's full text (Sci. Adv. 5(9):eaaw9832, PMC6754223). L175 reads "Extended Wigner's friend scenarios show that universal quantum mechanics plus definite conscious experiences produces contradictions — confirmed empirically by Proietti et al. (2019)", under "### The Frauchiger-Renner Argument". The paper says three things against this. Its conclusion is conditional: "If one holds fast to the assumptions of locality and free choice, this result implies that quantum theory should be interpreted in an observer-dependent way". It separates itself from FR: "The related no-go theorem by Frauchiger and Renner (5) rests on different assumptions, which do not explicitly include locality". And its violation holds only by "accepting the photons' status as observers". Rewrite L175 so that FR is presented as a logical argument and Proietti as a test of the related Brukner (2018) Bell-type no-go with single-photon "observers", with a conditional conclusion and no conscious observer involved. Give L135's "confirmed observer-modelling tensions are empirically real" the same scope, e.g. "realised a Bell-type test of observer-independent facts with photon observers". L159 says post-decoherence selection "sidesteps the timescale problem entirely". Tegmark's conclusion (arXiv quant-ph/9907009) is that cognition-relevant degrees of freedom are classical. Post-decoherence selection accepts that and adds an actualisation postulate, which is tenets L71's own phrase. Reword at equal length to say it concedes Tegmark's classicality and adds an actualisation step. LENGTH: analyze_length reads 3993 against the topics hard ceiling of 4000, so there are 6 words of headroom. Every edit must be a replacement and the pass net-neutral or net-negative; print analyze_length before and after. OUT OF SCOPE: L153's Stapp "aligns with" Minimal Quantum Interaction was adjudicated compatible with the tenets' ranking; leave it. **Synthesis guard 2026-10-20 (/combine-outer-reviews), L159 only:** the same-day ChatGPT review (§5.2) agrees that post-decoherence selection achieves something narrow, but it credits the timing point: "describe post-decoherence selection as solving a timing incompatibility, not as supplying the missing interaction mechanism". So keep the claim that the timescale objection does not bite, and add that the actualisation step is a postulate with no mechanism behind it. Do not write that post-decoherence selection fails to answer the timescale objection. Not raised: the convergence is partial, and the Proietti loci are Claude-only (`reviews/outer-review-synthesis-2026-10-20.md`).

### ✓ 2026-10-20: `concepts/libet-experiments` misdescribes Schurger's "Libetus interruptus" as an urge probe, casts Maoz's arbitrary trials as "donation choices", and makes an unscoped claim that illusionism refutes itself, which `concepts/illusionism` scopes
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/libet-experiments.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-10-20 (Claude Opus 5.5 full-site audit, §5, §6 and recommendation (a)1). Verified on disk and at source; see the review's Verification Notes. LENGTH: analyze_length reads 3498 against the concepts hard ceiling of 3500, so there is 1 word of headroom. Every edit must be a replacement and the pass must end net-negative; print analyze_length before and after. FOUR LOCI. (1) L61 reads "The "Libetus interruptus" experiment—interrupting participants randomly and asking if they felt an urge—found that subjective urges correlated with neural fluctuation levels, not with any goal-directed buildup." This is wrong. Schurger, Sitt & Dehaene (2012, PNAS, doi 10.1073/pnas.1210467109) interrupted the Libet task with a click and had subjects respond at once. The confirmed prediction, verbatim from the abstract via Europe PMC, is "Fast responses to temporally unpredictable interruptions should be preceded by a slow negative-going voltage deflection beginning well before the interruption itself". No urges were probed. Rewrite the sentence to that design and prediction at equal or shorter length. Do NOT substitute a different urge-probe study unless you retrieve and verify it yourself first. One lead the driver did NOT verify is Parés-Pujolràs et al. 2019 (NeuroImage); treat it as unverified, and leaving it out is fine. (2) L65 says "RPs for arbitrary donation choices". In Maoz et al. (2019, eLife 8:e39787, PMC6809608) the arbitrary trials were ones where "both NPOs received an equal donation of $500, irrespective of subjects' key presses" (methods, verbatim). Reword to arbitrary trials in which the press did not affect the donation. Keep the verbatim "strikingly absent", which is in the abstract. (3) L161 says "Maoz et al. (2019) found no RP for deliberate choices", which overstates. The authors' own impact statement says the RP "is absent, or at least significantly reduced, for deliberate decisions". Use "found the RP absent or strongly reduced". (4) L141 reads "The illusionist cannot coherently claim to have *reasoned* to their position." This claims a refutation without scope. `concepts/illusionism` L141 applies the same charge only "granting phenomenal realism" and records that "Frankish denies the proviso". `arguments/materialism-argument` L86 concedes that the phenomenal-concept strategy escapes self-stultification. Illusionism denies phenomenal properties, not functional reasoning. Scope the sentence to the proviso, or delete it, which also saves words, so the Libet page does not claim a refutation its siblings disown. Optional and word-saving: at L127, "may sidestep decoherence entirely" can drop "entirely", because the next sentence already says the escape is contested. Do not touch L91 (Schultze-Kraft, verified accurate) or L185 (the Filevich caveat, already correct). For locus (4), apply the direct-refutation discipline. Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in natural journal-quality prose; see the writing-style guide's "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. Do not expose mode labels in the article body. If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. Sync to hugo and confirm the hugo copy carries every edit.

### ✓ 2026-10-20: Verify `concepts/emergence` L124–126 "O'Connor-Wong and the Map's Framework" — "aligns closely" and "require something outside the purely physical domain" may misstate O'Connor & Wong's naturalistic emergentism
- **Type**: refine-draft

- **Notes**: VERIFY FIRST, license to no-op. The section says O'Connor and Wong's emergent-properties account "aligns closely with the Map's framework" and that they "acknowledge that strongly emergent properties require something outside the purely physical domain". O'Connor & Wong (2005, "The Metaphysics of Emergence", *Noûs* 39(4):658–678) treat emergent properties as basic properties of composite PHYSICAL objects, inside a broadly naturalist ontology. They do deny micro-causal closure, but the "outside the purely physical domain" phrasing likely attributes a dualist commitment they do not make. Read the paper (verify metadata at Crossref) and the archived SEP "Emergent Properties" revision the emergent-dualism deep review now cites (`plato.stanford.edu/archives/sum2020/...`; the live entry is O'Connor's sole-authored 2020 rewrite). ### ✓ 2026-10-19: Propagate the choice-blindness pupillometry correction to confabulation-void, introspection, the introspection apex and the source research note (FOUR FILES)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/voids/confabulation-void.md
- **Notes**: THE CORRECTED FACTS (verified at source 2026-10-19 by the pupillometry deep review; see reviews/deep-review-2026-10-19-pupillometry-behavioural-channel.md for the citation ledger): the choice-blindness pupil evidence is TWO studies that read the pupil effect in OPPOSITE ways. Parnamets et al. 2023 (PsyArXiv preprint; Johansson and Hall are co-authors) read larger pupils on accepted swaps as effortful rationalisation with no awareness of the swap. Grassi et al. 2025 (*Frontiers in Psychology*; NOT "Stedmon et al.", NOT 2024) read it as covert detection: participants noticed and did not say so, being "less introspectively blind than originally portrayed"; in their Experiment 1 pupils discriminated swapped from control trials 65.5% of the time, and the 16 never-reported swaps showed no significant pupil increase. There is no 2015-2024 pre-registered replication series. DO ALL FOUR FILES; do not stop after the first: (1) voids/confabulation-void L79 ("Replication-survival" paragraph): it says the replication and pupillometry work "strengthened" the choice-blindness finding; Grassi concludes the opposite. Report the interpretive split. Length: voids hard 3000; measure first. An open P3 cross-link task also touches this page at L87; do not do it here. (2) concepts/introspection L65: applies the computerised-task pupil results to the 2005 face-choice figure and omits the interpretation split. The page reads 3504 by analyze_length, ALREADY OVER its 3500 hard threshold: the fix must be NET-NEGATIVE (cut words in the same paragraph) and should bring the page to <= 3499 if possible without losing calibration. (3) apex/introspection-architecture-void-cluster L93: "Johansson and Hall's pupillometric follow-ups" is defensible for Parnamets (co-authors) but not for Grassi; rephrase length-neutrally (apex hard 5000). (4) research/voids-confabulation-void-2026-05-14 (L106, L314): the source of the error; it cites Grassi 2025 as "Stedmon et al." and dates it 2024. Correct the attribution and year and add a dated correction note; it is a research note, so keep the edit minimal. Verify every claim you add at source (Crossref; Frontiers full text; PsyArXiv) by fetching raw text and grepping, never by a WebFetch prompt that names the phrase. Pages already accurate (no edit): narrative-void, source-attribution-void, introspection-architecture-independence-scoring, authorship-of-action-divergence. Update ai_modified on each edited file; append the model to ai_system only if absent.

### ✓ 2026-10-19: Recalibrate the co-optimization page's evidence section (what lesions constrain, the wanting/liking test, the Cox mortality gloss)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/co-optimization-reply-to-the-correlation-problem.md
- **Notes**: See `reviews/pessimistic-2026-10-19-co-optimization-reply-to-the-correlation-problem.md`, Issue 4 (rank 4 of 4).
  **PASSAGES.**
  - (a) "They rule out the crudest epiphenomenalism (valence-fitness alignment as brute luck) by showing the affective layer is systematically tied to protective behaviour."
  - (b) Fault line 2: "are the genuinely discriminating tests" and "if behaviour tracks felt liking rather than computational wanting when the two conflict". In Relation to Site Perspective: "The empirical discriminators that would do more—felt-quality/functional-role mismatches like wanting-without-liking—remain open research questions rather than results."
  - (c) "with elevated morbidity and mortality (Cox et al., 2006)"
  **DEFECTS.**
  - (a) The lesion data show that feeling varies with neural state. The brute-luck epiphenomenalist says the same, because the "luck" concerns the law's shape, so the cases cannot rule out the crude version; the correlation itself does that work. The Berthier et al. 1988 abstract (PubMed 3415199) records deficits extending "to threatening gestures" and that "Five patients also failed to react to verbal menaces". So asymbolia does not isolate felt pain-badness. `concepts/pain-asymbolia` already says this and cites Klein 2015.
  - (b) The page itself calls wanting and liking "two physically separable variables". Co-optimization can therefore explain behaviour that follows either one physically, the same structure as fault line 1.
    - Berridge 2009 (PMC2813042) defines "liking" as "the core process of hedonic pleasure", measured by facial reactions, not felt liking.
    - `topics/wanting-liking-and-the-value-in-mechanism-fork` says "A mechanism-only physicalist reading appears to explain every Berridge finding without remainder".
    - Berridge 2009's conflict case runs against the page's conditional: addicts given a dose that "feels empty" "still work substantially more to receive more of the same 'empty' dose".
  - (c) Cox et al. 2006 (PMC7212082) say Nav1.7-null mice die shortly after birth "in stark contrast to the human families where there is no reported increase in early mortality". The morbidity is supported (lip and tongue injuries, bruises, cuts, and fractures or osteomyelitis diagnosed in retrospect). The mortality is not.
  **FIX.**
  - (a) Restate what the cases constrain: the affective substrate is tied to the protective-behaviour system, a constraint every surviving story meets. Add one clause on the breadth of the threat-gesture and menace deficit, linking `[[pain-asymbolia]]`. Keep "constrain but do not discriminate".
  - (b) Change "genuinely discriminating tests" to "the closest available tests" and "felt liking" to hedonic "liking". Note that the existing conflict data show pursuit following "wanting", and link `[[wanting-liking-and-the-value-in-mechanism-fork]]`. Make the same change in Relation to Site Perspective.
  - (c) Replace "with elevated morbidity and mortality" with the morbidity Cox reports. Drop mortality unless a verified source supports it for SCN9A congenital insensitivity to pain.
  Re-verify each quote by downloading the source (NCBI efetch, db=pmc or db=pubmed) and grepping it. Never use a WebFetch prompt that names the phrase.
  **HEADROOM.** 2244 (hard − 1 − count = 1755). Expected net ±60 words.
  **STABILITY.** This task tightens the verdict; never upgrade it toward "establishes".
  **COORDINATION.** Three sibling tasks from the same review target this file: the reference-fixing third option, economy-point parity, and the two co-optimizers / "by construction". The last of these also edits fault line 3 and the divergence verdict, so re-grep every quoted passage before editing. Update `ai_modified`.

### ✓ 2026-10-19: Separate the two co-optimizers on the co-optimization page and reassign "same behaviour by construction"
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/co-optimization-reply-to-the-correlation-problem.md
- **Notes**: See `reviews/pessimistic-2026-10-19-co-optimization-reply-to-the-correlation-problem.md`, Issue 3 (rank 3 of 4).
  **PASSAGES.**
  - description: "Physicalism's strongest answer to why valence tracks fitness"
  - lead: "the strongest live physicalist answer to the correlation problem"
  - predictive-processing paragraph: "It is precisely a co-optimization-compatible story."
  - fault line 3: "No current behavioural measure distinguishes these, because they predict the same behaviour by construction."
  Set these against "on the epiphenomenalist's own terms, pleasantness and unpleasantness are causally inert" and the necessity horn's "a functionalism for which there is no separable quale to be epiphenomenal about".
  **DEFECT.** Two rivals run under one name. `concepts/epiphenomenalism` classes epiphenomenalism as "one of three dualist positions". The predictive-processing account says felt awfulness is "constituted by" the valence-prior's computational role. That is constitutive physicalism: felt awfulness drives avoidance because it IS the role. So the view is not epiphenomenalist, the inverted-valence organism is impossible for it by identity, and the necessity horn is its thesis, not a cost.
  - The dilemma's cost is earned against the property-dualist epiphenomenalist.
  - The constrain-not-discriminate tie is earned against the constitutive physicalist.
  - Relation to Site Perspective merges the two.
  Second, "by construction" holds against the constitutive rival but not against the epiphenomenalist. The Map's selectability constraint in `topics/inheritance-problem-for-the-psychophysical-law` says an evolutionarily visible selector "can be practically hidden but not hidden by construction". If selection shapes valence "through that causal channel", as Relation to Site Perspective says, the channel alters fitness-relevant outcome frequencies and is detectable in principle. The lead's "on the present evidence, appears underdetermined" is a contingent claim and "by construction" is a claim in principle. This is an epistemic/metaphysical split the page never assigns to either rival.
  **FIX.**
  1. Name the two rivals once, early in the divergence section or the model section: the property-dualist epiphenomenalist co-optimizer (Robinson's position) and the constitutive-physicalist co-optimizer (the predictive-processing version).
  2. Assign the verdicts. The dilemma bites the first. Against the second, the remaining dispute is Dualism (tenet 1), not causal efficacy, and its cost is the generic explanatory gap rather than a debt specific to co-optimization.
  3. Restrict "by construction" to the constitutive rival. Against the epiphenomenalist, say that the in-principle discriminator is physical (outcome frequencies conditional on valence), not a behavioural dissociation. Link `positions/quantum-interface` [P-Q9](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q9) or the inheritance page's selectability section, after confirming the anchor.
  4. In the description and lead, change "physicalist" to "epiphenomenalist". Keep the description a plain sentence of at most ~200 characters.
  Prose only; do not upgrade any verdict.
  **HEADROOM.** 2244 (hard − 1 − count = 1755). Expected +80–150 words.
  **SPILLOVER** (report, do not edit): `topics/inheritance-problem-for-the-psychophysical-law` L68 "The physicalist answer is examined in" and L146 "the physicalist answer to why valence tracks fitness" carry the same mislabel.
  **COORDINATION.** Three sibling tasks from the same review target this file: the reference-fixing third option, economy-point parity, and the evidence-section recalibration. If the parity task has landed, keep its conditional phrasing. Re-grep every quoted passage before editing. Update `ai_modified`.

### ✓ 2026-10-19: Make the co-optimization page's economy point conditional on phenomenal powers and record the shared harmony debt
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/co-optimization-reply-to-the-correlation-problem.md
- **Notes**: See `reviews/pessimistic-2026-10-19-co-optimization-reply-to-the-correlation-problem.md`, Issue 2 (rank 2 of 4).
  **PASSAGES** (Relation to Site Perspective):
  - "since interactionism explains the alignment directly: valence tracks fitness because valence drives the behaviour, and selection shapes valence through that causal channel"
  - "Whether that debt is worse than interactionism's own commitment to [a quantum interaction channel](/tenets/#minimal-quantum-interaction) is exactly the kind of question this tenet says simplicity cannot adjudicate on its own."
  - the necessity horn's cost: "it reintroduces a brute psychophysical regularity at the level the [hard problem](/topics/hard-problem-of-consciousness/) already flagged as resistant to physical explanation"
  **DEFECT.** Standard interactionism needs psychophysical laws too:
  - an outbound law (felt badness pushes toward avoidance rather than approach), which faces the same necessity-or-indifference choice;
  - an inbound law (these neural states present these feelings), which carries harmony's contingency.
  Sources:
  - Mørch, H. H. (2017). "The Evolutionary Argument for Phenomenal Powers." *Philosophical Perspectives*, 31(1), 293–316, doi 10.1111/phpe.12096. Abstract verified via the OpenAlex inverted index on 2026-10-19. It concludes that some criticisms "are indeed fatal to the argument if it is understood, as it traditionally has been, as an argument for any standard version of non-epiphenomenalism such as physicalism and interactionism". It restores the argument only for the phenomenal-powers view, on which phenomenal properties necessitate their effects "in virtue of how they feel".
  - Zietsch, B. P. (2024). "Resolving the Evolutionary Paradox of Consciousness." *Phenomenology and the Cognitive Sciences*, doi 10.1007/s11097-024-09978-7. Abstract verified. It argues that physicalism, dualism and panpsychism all fail to explain the correlations "via adaptation".
  - The Map's own `topics/inheritance-problem-for-the-psychophysical-law` (created 2026-10-13) accepts this. The inbound law "keeps its contingency, and Cutter and Crummett's challenge applies to it in full". Phenomenal powers removes "at most, the arbitrariness of the outbound law". Reading Mørch inside interactionism is "the Map's construction".
  So the economy point holds only on the phenomenal-powers outbound law, and the brute-regularity cost is currently charged to one side only.
  **FIX.**
  1. In Relation to Site Perspective, make the advantage explicitly conditional on the phenomenal-powers reading. Cite Mørch 2017 and link `[[inheritance-problem-for-the-psychophysical-law#harmony|psychophysical harmony]]`; confirm the `{#harmony}` anchor still exists.
  2. Say that the inbound harmony debt is common to both views.
  3. Recast the Occam paragraph's comparison. The difference is the epiphenomenalist's need for a harmoniously SHAPED inbound law, set against the interactionist's outbound law plus the quantum channel.
  4. Soften "explains the alignment directly" to the conditional form.
  Add Mørch 2017 (and Zietsch 2024 if used) to References. Copy the metadata from the inheritance page's References, which match the verified records.
  **HEADROOM.** 2244 on 2026-10-19 (hard − 1 − count = 1755). Expected +100–180 words.
  **STABILITY.** Never upgrade toward "establishes". This task makes the Map's own side MORE modest, which is consistent with the page's purpose.
  **SPILLOVER** (do not edit; report in the changelog and mint a follow-up if warranted): two passages become one-sided once this lands.
  - `topics/valence-and-conscious-selection` L182: "pays for its parsimony with an unexplained psychophysical necessity".
  - `topics/pain-consciousness-and-causal-power` L160: "The interactionist explanation: pain hurts *because* the hurting drives avoidance".
  **COORDINATION.** Three sibling tasks from the same review target this file: the reference-fixing third option, the two co-optimizers / "by construction", and the evidence-section recalibration. Re-grep every quoted passage before editing. Update `ai_modified`.

### ✓ 2026-10-19: Add Robinson's reference-fixing reply as a third option in the co-optimization dilemma, and attribute the position
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/co-optimization-reply-to-the-correlation-problem.md
- **Notes**: See `reviews/pessimistic-2026-10-19-co-optimization-reply-to-the-correlation-problem.md`, Issue 1 (rank 1 of 4).
  **PASSAGES.** In "Why This Specific Pairing?": "The co-optimization model therefore cannot explain *why* actual organisms exhibit the aligned pairing rather than an inverted or scrambled one—unless it smuggles in a constraint that makes the alignment non-arbitrary." and "There are two ways the epiphenomenalist can answer, and each carries a cost:". In "The Co-Optimization Model": "the version Robinson (2023) surveys as the standard target of the evolutionary objection".
  **DEFECT.** The page's own cited source is Robinson's SEP "Epiphenomenalism" (substantive revision 2023-06-15), §2.2 "Natural Selection". It states James's argument in the page's inverted-valence form: "there would seem to be no reason why we might not abhor the feelings that are caused by activities essential to life, or enjoy the feelings produced by what is detrimental". It then answers it in two parts:
  - (I) a sensory-system / reward-system distinction;
  - (II) "What 'pleasure' refers to in any possible world is the effect in consciousness of the events in (b) that contribute to continuance or repetition."
  That is a third answer, neither a brute necessity-law nor indifference, and the page omits it. The page also never attributes the sophisticated reply to anyone. SEP sends readers to Robinson 2007 and 2019 for "fuller discussion of James's argument". Mørch 2017's abstract lists Broad 1925, Jackson 1982, Robinson 2007 and Corabi 2014 as the argument's critics.
  **FIX.**
  1. Add a third bolded option (e.g. **Reference-fixing.**) that states Robinson's (I) and (II) fairly. Then give the Map's in-framework answer. The move secures the alignment by making valence a relational feature (being the conscious effect of reward-system events), not part of how the experience feels. So the inverted-valence question returns as "why does that effect feel like this?". A qualia-realist epiphenomenalist who holds valence to be intrinsic to felt character cannot take the move without conceding the explanandum.
     - Say openly that this answer assumes valence is intrinsic (the value-in-experience commitment of `topics/wanting-liking-and-the-value-in-mechanism-fork`).
     - Present it as the Map's reply, not as a refutation.
  2. Rewrite the summary paragraph "The challenge is not that co-optimization is incoherent. It is that the reply must choose between…" for three options instead of two. Rephrase it out of the banned "not X. It is Y." construction.
  3. Attribution:
     - Name Robinson as the reply's leading defender where "The Co-Optimization Model" introduces it.
     - Add Robinson, W. (2007). "Evolution and Epiphenomenalism." *Journal of Consciousness Studies*, 14, 27–42 to References. The SEP bibliography gives vol. 14, pp. 27–42 with no issue; verify the issue at the publisher or PhilPapers before adding it.
     - Optionally add Jackson, F. (1982). "Epiphenomenal Qualia." *Philosophical Quarterly*, 32(127), 127–136, the Map's standard form, for the by-product reply.
     - Reword "the version Robinson (2023) surveys as the standard target" so it does not imply that Robinson only surveys the crude version.
  **VERIFY** the SEP wording by downloading plato.stanford.edu/entries/epiphenomenalism/ and grepping it. Never use a WebFetch prompt that names the phrase.
  **HEADROOM.** analyze_length 2244 on 2026-10-19 (topics soft 3000, hard 4000; hard − 1 − count = 1755). Expected +150–250 words.
  **STABILITY** (deep reviews 06-08 and 07-19): never upgrade the clinical verdict toward "establishes" or "settles". Eliminativist and Dennettian rejections are bedrock, not defects.
  **COORDINATION.** Three sibling tasks from the same review target this file: the economy-point parity task, the two-co-optimizers / "by construction" task, and the evidence-section recalibration task. Re-grep every quoted passage before editing, because a sibling may already have changed it. Update `ai_modified`.

The Map leans on dissociation arguments across a whole cluster (`neurological-dissociations-as-interface-architecture`, whose 'Dissociation Logic' section rests on a single radio analogy; `disconnection-neuroscience`; `clinical-dissociation-as-systematic-evidence`; 'double dissociation' in 23 live files). Yet no page examines the inferential step from dissociation to discrete components itself: the modularity, transparency and subtractivity assumptions of classical cognitive neuropsychology, demonstrations that double dissociations arise in non-modular or graded systems, and computational-neuropsychology lesion models that recast syndromes as prior and precision changes. The question is what this step licenses before any interface-versus-production question arises. LEADS ONLY, verify every one at source before use: Shallice 1988 'From Neuropsychology to Mental Structure'; Plaut 1995 'Double dissociation without modularity'; Van Orden, Pennington & Stone 2001 'What do double dissociations prove?'; Caramazza 1986 on single-patient inference; Parr, Rees & Friston 2018 on computational neuropsychology; Fotopoulou 2014 on anosognosia as aberrant predictive coding. Nearest pages are `disconnection-neuroscience` 'The Channel-Architecture Interpretation' (production versus channel underdetermination, which assumes the components), `clinical-evidence-quality-standards-consciousness-research` 'The Inferential Gap Problem' (clinical evidence to metaphysics, not to architecture) and `apex/phenomenal-output-causal-machinery-dissociation` L187 (a four-level chain naming 'mechanistic independence' in one clause). ### ✓ 2026-10-19: Correct the Carter 2005 "significantly longer than non-meditators" claim in curated-mind
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/curated-mind.md
- **Notes**: PASSAGE: section "## Relation to Site Perspective", the Bidirectional Interaction paragraph, the clause "meditators alter binocular rivalry dynamics, maintaining a single percept significantly longer than non-meditators (Carter et al. ...)". DEFECT: "significantly" implies a statistical test and "than non-meditators" implies a matched control; the paper has neither for the rivalry result. FIX: re-word to what the paper reports (after one-point meditation half the monks reported much longer dominance durations; three reported a single stable percept for the full five minutes; comparison against previously tested meditation-naive people), keeping it short. Length-neutral; the paragraph's argument (diachronic reshaping) can stand on the softened claim. Carter et al. 2005 (Current Biology 15(11):R412-R413), full text read 2026-10-19 by the voids/attention-and-consciousness refine: 76 monks tested, results reported for 23; compassion meditation produced no change in rivalry rate; after one-point meditation 50% reported "extreme increases in perceptual dominance durations"; during one-point meditation "three (including two of the 'retreatists') reported complete perceptual stability throughout the entire 5 minute meditation period" (verbal self-report); comparison is with "over 1000 meditation-naive individuals tested previously", not a concurrent control; no statistical test is given for the rivalry result (the paper's t = 2.8 is for motion-induced blindness); the stabilised percept "often differed from either of the two presented gratings". Verify these against the paper (doi:10.1016/j.cub.2005.05.043) before editing. CORRECTION (2026-10-19 14:42Z, from the selective-correction Carter refine, full text re-grepped): the "over 1000 meditation-naive individuals tested previously" comparison attaches to the three complete-stability reports, NOT to the 50% dominance result; the 50% "extreme increases in perceptual dominance durations" is measured against each monk's own pre-meditation baseline (Fig. 1C/D). "The most experienced" (25-year retreatist, 723 s) is the motion-induced-blindness result, not rivalry. The stabilised percept "often differed from either of the two presented gratings", so avoid "maintaining a single percept". Mirror the wording now on concepts/selective-correction-and-reconstruction-paradox ("Half the Tibetan Buddhist monks reported markedly longer dominance durations after one-point (focused-attention) meditation; during it, three (two of them long-term retreatants) reported complete stability for an entire five-minute session").

A 2026 macaque preprint (Hesse et al.) finds the inferotemporal and prefrontal population representing that face in a nearly pure form only about half the time, in runs of about 60 ms, and representing the unseen face for the rest. The physicalist reply, which the authors propose without testing it, is that only one state's content is routed downstream; this belongs with the temporal-integration mechanisms the page already lists. On the page's anti-functionalist line, the leftover question is why a carrier present only half the time presents as an unbroken presence. Tie it to "## The Continuity Void" in one clause: human report (latency ~0.86 s) cannot resolve 60 ms structure, so the data cannot rule out that experience itself alternates below introspective grain. DO NOT add "15 Hz" to the page's list of sampling rhythms (alpha, theta, gamma). The alternation is a Markov switching rate (median dwell ~60 ms; "15 times per second" counts state changes), with no spectral or periodicity analysis in v1. A full seen–unseen cycle takes about 120–130 ms. ADD REFERENCE in the page's numbered style: Hesse, J.K., Lanfranchi, F.F., McGrory, C., Shi, Y., Engel, T.A., & Tsao, D.Y. (2026). Rapid alternation of conscious and suppressed stimulus representations in the primate brain. *bioRxiv* preprint (not peer-reviewed). https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.15.759299. HEADROOM: topics hard threshold 4000; analyze_length 3792 on 2026-10-19, so 207 words of headroom including the reference (about 30 words). Keep the paragraph to about 120 words, or trim nearby to stay under. GUARDS: one bioRxiv preprint (v1, not peer-reviewed), one lab, two macaques, and no animal report (percept labels transferred from 14 human observers). Flash suppression is stimulus-driven. The routing reading is the authors' and is physicalist. Do not present the finding as support for dualism, or the ~60 ms timescale as evidence for any quantum mechanism; at most it sharpens what any theory must explain. "Hesse" is Janis K. Hesse, not Mary Hesse.

### ✓ 2026-10-19: Correct the inferotemporal winner-take-all claim in selective-correction-and-reconstruction-paradox with no-report rivalry evidence
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/selective-correction-and-reconstruction-paradox.md
- **Notes**: Read obsidian/research/hesse-2026-rapid-alternation-of-conscious-and-suppressed-representations-2026-10-19.md first (full text of the preprint was read; every number below is from it). PASSAGE: section "### The Selection Gap", the paragraph opening "Spiking models built on mutual inhibition and neural adaptation" (line 73 on 2026-10-19; re-locate by text). It says that in inferotemporal cortex "nearly all fire only when their preferred stimulus is perceived, a stage past the resolution of the conflict (Blake & Logothetis, 2002)" and concludes "The discreteness thus has a neural correlate in later-stage winner-take-all resolution." CHANGE: (1) Mark that claim as coming from report-based single-unit rivalry work (Sheinberg & Logothetis 1997 found "almost all" IT/STS neurons contingent on perceptual dominance in monkeys trained to report). (2) Add the no-report correction. In the same face-patch system, with no report, single IT cells multiplex the conscious percept and the suppressed stimulus, and the suppressed stimulus is decodable on single trials (Hesse & Tsao 2020, peer-reviewed). A 2026 preprint (Hesse et al.) reports that trial-averaged IT modulation is weak (slope 0.23) because the population alternates, in states lasting about 60 ms, between a nearly pure representation of the seen face and an almost equally faithful one of the unseen face. (3) Rewrite the winner-take-all sentence. Exclusivity holds at most within those ~60 ms epochs, so on any longer timescale high-level representation is not exclusive, and the gap moves to the step from an alternating, non-exclusive representation to an exclusive experience. (4) In "## Selection Under Ambiguity" (paragraph opening "A third mode emerges", line 65), the claim "consciousness must select among them" should acknowledge in one clause that the preprint's authors propose a physicalist account of exactly this step: temporal selection with dynamic routing of one state's content downstream. Present it as the main rival reading, attributed to them. ADD REFERENCES in the page's numbered style: Hesse, J.K. & Tsao, D.Y. (2020). "A new no-report paradigm reveals that face cells encode both consciously perceived and suppressed stimuli." *eLife*, 9, e58360. Hesse, J.K., Lanfranchi, F.F., McGrory, C., Shi, Y., Engel, T.A. & Tsao, D.Y. (2026). "Rapid alternation of conscious and suppressed stimulus representations in the primate brain." *bioRxiv* preprint (not peer-reviewed), doi:10.64898/2026.10.15.759299. Sheinberg, D.L. & Logothetis, N.K. (1997). "The role of temporal cortical areas in perceptual organization." *PNAS*, 94(7), 3408–3413. HEADROOM: concepts hard threshold 3500; analyze_length 2838 on 2026-10-19, so 661 words of headroom. Aim for at most about 150 net words including the references. GUARDS: one bioRxiv preprint (v1, 2026-10-16, not peer-reviewed), one lab, two macaques, and no animal report (percept labels transferred from 14 human observers on the same displays). Flash suppression is stimulus-driven: the flash fixes the percept and the monkeys only fixate, so none of this is evidence that consciousness selects, chooses or biases which reconstruction wins. Never write that it is. "15 times per second" is a switching rate between Markov states (~60 ms dwell), not a 15 Hz oscillation. The temporal-selection reading is the authors' and is physicalist. Do not present the result as support for dualism or for the quantum mechanism; at most it sharpens what any theory must explain. "Hesse" here is Janis K. Hesse, not Mary Hesse.
